### 18.27 Gap 24b merged (10 Sep 13:45, 577fe1d); the run inspection page; the owner's questions on context

**Owner's ruling (13:10): a protected hold makes a RECOGNISED file askable at site G** -- `00`:110 ("the LLM should not be called for direct, unique matches") yields to a hold, because the hold is a word-list guess about what the file IS and the local model reads the whole text. Built and merged: `precaution_report` takes a `Recognition` or an `Abstention`, and all three writers of `basis='safety_domain'` share one projection (`_reported`), so a hold and its report cannot disagree; `SituationQuestion.recognised_as` beside a nullable `reason`, exactly one set, and `recognised_as` is refused without a hold (`00`:110 still stands where no hold exists); the shortlist for a recognised file is the recognised schema, the held domain, and the decline; the situation pass settles only a file recognised AND unheld. Verified on the merged head: 267 passed (189 recognition, 8 new integration, the gap 24, coverage, boundary and end-to-end files). **Owed:** G's protected row on a CONFIRMED hold cites the winner's evidence, not the hold's (`Precaution` carries no refs); the `--semantic-model` defect from §18.26 stands.

**The owner could not see what the product does (13:30).** There is no surface that shows, for one file, what was extracted, what the model was sent, what it answered, what the validator did, how it was classified and where it went; the CLI prints summaries and review questions and the scorer prints scores. Interim: a local script outside the repo (`~/.graph-agent/lead/inspect_run.py`) renders a run database as that walk (`r19-inspect.md`, `r20-inspect.md`; nothing leaves the machine). **Gap 25 (new, product):** the person can open any file's trail -- extracted → sent → answered → judged → classified → placed -- from the product itself; `00`'s "a person can see why" has no surface today.

**Answered for the owner (13:35):** the model sees the file's own zones, its name, sibling context lines, the situation's folder levels with their meanings, the glossary, the closed vocabulary, the readings and the conflicts flag; it does NOT see other files' answers, the group, or a graph (gaps 12, 14). The template becomes the levels; the domain's fields become site A's questions; its terms feed the detector; site C chooses among rule-built legal folders. An agent loop was declined for the design's own reasons (audit, bounded release, replay); the context the model lacks is to be added in the same shape: gap 14 first after r21, then site E.

### 18.28 The parallel cloud lane merged (10 Sep 16:05, b3f0f17); the afternoon outage; R-176 opened

**Owner (13:55-14:05): build gap 14 and site E now; fix every identified gap in parallel.** Five agents dispatched (cloud lane, gap 14, site E + S6, gaps 18+19, gaps 20+22+23); the next wave (gaps 16+11b, gap 25, the semantic-model defect at G, gap 12) queued behind them, because five test sessions beside the resident model is the stall condition of 11:35.

**The cloud lane (§18.15) merged.** The round trip is a suspension point: the transport spends the release, writes the audit row, and yields a pending send holding only the finished bytes; `harness.in_walk_order` advances every subject on the calling thread in walk order, performs a batch of cloud sends in a thread pool, and resumes the batch in walk order, so every database write stays serial and ordered. The count is `cli.EXTRACTION_WORKERS`, the one place the product already chooses how many things read at once; site C receives it through `PipelineInputs.calls_at_once`. A local send is never parked. **Found and fixed on the way:** R-175's clock closed the local file's turn when sends were parked and nothing reopened it, so the one call the per-file ceiling exists to bound was charged to nobody (120 s charged with the fix, 0 without). One P8 egress pin had gone blind (matched only a bare `model_client.invoke`) and was re-argued. Verified on the merged head: 1,037 passed (3 xfailed) across the new lane file, the local fact pass, the site G files, p8, coverage, the ceiling and the two-runs-agree file. **Not built, sent back to the same agent:** the local send should run WHILE the cloud batch is in flight (flush-before-local shrinks batches toward one on an interleaved corpus); within-batch duplicate files do not reuse each other's answer (prior-run reuse unaffected).

**The outage (14:15-15:45).** The internet dropped for ~90 minutes: three agents died (one on DNS, two on the stream watchdog) and were resumed with their worktrees intact; r20 made seven site A dossiers in that window and recorded NO failure, because the cloud client has no whole-call deadline. **R-176 (open, dispatched with the follow-on):** `readers/model_deepseek` gets one deadline over the whole call, by phase, mirroring R-175, derived from the deployment's existing cloud timeout.

### 18.29 Gaps 18 and 19 merged (10 Sep 16:15, 92be8f4)

**Gap 18.** `readers/image_headers` reads §2.6's metadata slots through ImageIO via `Quartz`, which the deployment already ships for Vision (nothing added); properties only, no image decoded. HEIC/HEIF/AVIF/TIFF/BMP take their branch from the product's own signature table (`signatures.format_from_magic` publishes the byte half of `detect_format`; one implementation). Tag classification by Quartz's own constants, no tag table. Tier 1 is §2.6's five nouns only (make, model, lens, ISO, focal length): ranking the whole EXIF dict tier 1 would have made a macOS screenshot "strong photo evidence" beside its tier-3 PNG format, and §2.6's conflicting-signals rule would abstain on the very file `photos.screenshot-captures` exists for -- measured and pinned. Facts land as `metadata`-zone observations addressed `field/<tag>`, the `metadata_field_name` citation form; one name, one row. A BMP records no unread reason, honestly: the reader cannot tell a container with no EXIF box from a stripped photograph, and P4 forbids writing either as evidence. `image.metadata` 0.1.0 → 0.2.0.

**Gap 19.** `VISION_CONFIG` no longer carries `["en-US"]`; `recognition_languages()` asks Vision for its published set at the configured level and `_recognise` enables the OS's language identification so the FILE decides. **The measurement that decided it:** on rendered CJK text, `en-US` reads nothing, the whole published set with detection OFF reads nothing, detection ON reads it at confidence 1.0 -- the detection flag is the load-bearing line, not a longer list. On an older macOS without that flag the gap stays open, said in code. Every stored OCR and image fingerprint changes, so the next run re-reads images (intended).

Verified on the merged head: 183 passed in 76.51s (0:01:16). Next wave dispatched into the freed slot: gaps 16 + 11b.

### 18.30 The local call runs beside the cloud lane; r21 launched seeded from r20; R-176 dispatched (10 Sep 16:15-16:35)

**Merged (a5de067).** `harness._settle` submits a batch's cloud sends to the pool, walks the batch's LOCAL sends on the calling thread inside that window, then gathers the cloud answers -- one thread performs every local send, so two local sends cannot overlap (r18's swap was two local calls), and a cloud socket waiting beside one costs the local server nothing. A local send joins a batch only when cloud work is already parked; with nothing in the air it settles inline, so a run with no cloud target is byte-identical. The batch width counts every parked send (a parked send is a spent release holding a budget slot). R-175's clock: pause before the window, resume per subject before its own send, pause after the last local one so the wait for cloud answers is billed to nobody. Verified on the merged head: 48 passed in 835.24s (0:13:55).

**A lead error.** During the §18.29 cleanup the lead removed the cloud-lane agent's worktree while it was still building the follow-ons. Its first follow-on was already committed and survived on its branch (merged above); R-176 had been researched, not written. **R-176 dispatched fresh** with the research handed over: keep the SDK (the one-import-site pin, and no hand-rolled TLS on the key's path); the seam is `OpenAI(http_client=)` with a transport that sizes each phase's timeout from the remaining budget and checks between body chunks; the number stays `cli.MODEL_CALL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`; the open question (whether httpcore binds the read timer once, making a trickle-then-stall overshoot by one budget) to be settled and documented before writing.

**r20 stopped, r21 launched (16:17, 6511468).** r20 was serial at site A on the old head (two cloud dossiers in nine minutes); r21 runs the merged head (cloud lane, gaps 24/24b, R-175, 18/19) with `--reuse-answers-from` r20, whose 18 fact answers were copied in; the situation pass reruns (gap 24 changed held files' dossiers). Load at launch 142-183 with five agents' test sessions beside it; nothing more dispatched until reports land.

**Gap 11b, the tree half (agent's question, lead's answer).** Minting a scoped General under every supported parent would answer a question `00`:99 leaves deliberately open (`cli.py:9927` passes `()` with the recorded argument "an unasked question answered by default is a folder nobody wanted"). The decision side is built (an offered ancestor chosen becomes a placement at that depth; an existing scoped-general child is on the menu). **Owed to the owner:** mint on demand, only under a parent that has a file whose facts support the parent and no leaf, which needs the tree pass to see placement outcomes -- a product ordering question.

**Gap 14 merged (81f07e1, seven conflicts against the cloud lane resolved by keeping both: the group's own call first, then every member through the lane with the group's answer attached).** One site C dossier whose subject is the GROUP (`group:<id>`; the members' accepted facts, the union of their legal candidates scored by the existing scoring, the same levels, vocabulary, policy and ratified text); a member whose own stated values do not rule the group's folder out goes with it and makes no call (`decided_by=model`, `group_support` written for the first time); a member that contradicts it (§6.3's suppression or P9's outlier flag) is judged singly with the group's folder offered as a set-aside candidate and the disagreement recorded; one destination every member may use (cloud only if every member routes cloud; withheld if the gate refuses any member). ### 18.33 Gaps 16 + 11b and gap 25 merged; three candidate rows written (10 Sep 19:05-19:20)

**Gaps 16 + 11b merged (a0e389a; 853 passed, 1 xfailed on the merged head).** `index._chain_around` walks up and down from the reached nodes off the two indexes the schema already declares (`parent_node_id` joined to the term sources so both directions are answerable without a whole-tree scan); UP suppresses (a value on an ancestor is one the file acquires by being filed below it), DOWN explains (a conflicting value below rules that descendant out and is named); the tree is canonicalised once per build and the subject's facts once per file through `cli.normalize_for_model`, the validator's own canonicaliser, at build time because the module's three reads are SQL equalities; `ConflictConsidered.found_on` names where. 11b: `_levels_not_filled` writes SPEC:401-404's field for the first time (six writers had said `()`), so a placement on an offered ancestor records the levels between it and the leaf; a contender's own scoped-general child is on the shortlist as a set-aside, offered and never scored. Three pins flipped with their reasons; the r37 fixture recaptured for one new key after a structural diff of exactly six differences. **The merge needed one thing the agent could not see:** `retrieve` and `build_destination_index` now take `canonical=`, and two call sites written after its base (gap 14's group retrieval, the group-call pin's index build) lacked it -- 35 failures, 9 errors, two lines. **Owed to the owner, restated:** the on-demand General under a parent that has a parent-only-supported file (a tree-pass ordering question).

**Gap 25 merged (555fc83; 152 passed).** `review_surface/trail.py` `file_trail(conn, wanted)` walks the five stages from rows only; `--trail FILE` beside `--explain`, which now points at it in one sentence. ### 18.40 Q-H and R-177 merged (11 Sep 09:40-09:55 local)

**Q-H merged (a21f836; 170 passed, 6 xfailed on its set).** Nothing in `src/` read `VerticalOption.unresolved_file_ids`; the placement pass and the freeze were already giving a kept branch's members no destination (measured on the decision rows: `abstain`, no `place`). The change is the option's own record: `opt_no_split` now carries every member as unresolved, which is what the owner's ruling says a kept branch is. R-27's strict xfail asserted the rejected hypothesis (`placed_after >= placed_before`) and could never pass under the ruling; replaced by a pin that the kept branch files none of its members (red-first: `0 >= 3`). The option is selected by position, so nothing about which option is chosen moved; the r37 fixture did not change.

**R-177 merged (7efa4fa; 649 passed on its set).** `readers/model_ollama.py` streams (`"stream": True`); a second clock beside R-175's budget, `quiet_since`, is reset by every complete NDJSON line and armed with the nearer of the two remainders; silence starts at request-sent. Its own class, `OllamaRanOutOfTimeWaitingForAToken`, under `OllamaRanOutOfTime`; `CALL_PHASES` stays four (a second clock, not a fifth phase). `assemble(raw)` joins the pieces with no field computed, dropped or reordered, so a one-object body round-trips byte-identically. **The silence deadline is `cli.MODEL_CALL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (90 s), injected as R-176 injects it into the cloud client** -- that number has always been how long this deployment lets a model say nothing; the local number (600 s) stays the whole-answer bound. Four red-first pins against a loopback server, two sabotages run (the deadline never consulted: two pins fail at 30 s against a 4 s deadline; the clock armed once: the trickle pin fails). **Exposure, the owner's lever:** the token stream is legitimately silent for the whole prompt read; at the measured ~105 tok/s, 90 s covers ~9,400 prompt tokens, and under load that rate falls -- a long prompt read on a loaded machine would be recorded as a hang. The lever is the injected number in `cli.py`, not code; nothing chosen here. **Owed:** the scorecard's `call_failure` line tallies by `failure_class`, which is `client_raised` for every reader exception, so R-175 and R-177 print as one number there; the discriminating name is in the row's explanation and `--trail` prints it. r23b runs at 88b883d and is unaffected.

### 18.42 The design audit of 11 Sep (00, 66, 75 against head 550f542): what held, where the misses concentrate, and wave 3

The owner asked that nothing in the original product design be missed before Phase 4, and that Phase 5's "the user decides the high-level folders first" be built now. A read-only audit (twelve Opus auditors, one per design area, each cross-checked against this register; the full twelve tables are the lead's scratch file, outside the repo) read `00` in full, `66` §§1-22 and `75`, and classified every commitment BUILT / PARTIAL / ABSENT / CONTRADICTED with `file:function` and test.

**What held.** Protected containers marked, counted, never opened, never silently omitted, from the path shape before any stat. The provenance log append-only with all nineteen named events. Identity, staleness, the four verification points, collision detection and conditional undo built and tested. Facet extraction with word-boundary matching, positional weighting, score and margin. Date parsing refuses fuzzy matching by raising. The 2026-09-05 amendments honoured: the validator flags rather than vetoes; placement routes through the model. Per area: extraction ~70 of 82 built; facts and schemas ~40 of 56 (23 schemas, 20 with fields, 19 templated, four safety domains behaving as such); trust and undo genuinely built; onboarding 13 of `75`'s 19 tasks.

**Where the misses concentrate -- three places.**
1. **The person is absent from the decisions the design gives them.** `00`:66-67: "let them decide the major branches first, and then progressively design each accepted branch"; "the tree-design process begins horizontally at the top level". CONTRADICTED in sequence: `tree_design/pipeline.py` computes the horizontal candidates AFTER filtering them by an answer supplied in advance; `cli.design_decisions()` supplies the accepted group ids and `surface=SURFACE_UNATTENDED`; `nesting_chooser` takes `options[0]` before the person sees the counts (R-92); `review_and_accept` and `approve_plan` accept everything `decided_by=RULES` (SF-3). Seven of the eight canvas actions have no writer; branch cards (file count, groups, related folders, explanation, node type, sensitive marker) live on `BranchCandidate` and no screen renders them; "evidence gaps" have no producer; the tree health view has no caller.
2. **Safety and quality predicates wired to constants that make them unfireable.** `screen_metadata=lambda: ()` at both `FactResolver` constructions (so `python-docx` and `Mozilla/5.0` become facts, `00`:28 CONTRADICTED, though `facts/discount.py` is built, catalogue-driven and tested); `near_match=lambda: False`; `materially_improves_retrieval=lambda: True`; `sensitive_group_ids=frozenset()`; `residual_handling_class=lambda: ORDINARY_CLASS`; `RESIDUAL_MAX_DEPTH=0` overwriting every template's authored depth; `template_request_for(... conflicts=())`; the archive router carrying one token; two exclusion categories empty.
3. **Finished, tested code with no caller**: `group_plan_review_item`, `review_surface/residual.py` (per-set cards computed and printed nowhere), `IndexEntry.anchor_excerpt_keys` (zero readers), the six template validations (never run on a generated template), `tree_health`, `placement/review.py:apply_review_action` (custom folders). A green suite describes parts nobody can reach; R-45's census is this finding in numbers.

**Placement misses the judge in two places:** `_multi_home_decision` writes a `place` with `decided_by=DECIDED_BY_RULE` and never reaches site C (against the amendment); after the group's own answer a member is filed AT `group_answer.node_id` verbatim, never sub-classified inside the branch (`00`:112). The dossier carries no `missing_fields` and no score values (`00`:110). Structural relationships (version family, duplicate family, archive family, photo event) have a retrieval weight and no producer; `version_family` and the near-duplicate pairing are deliberately uncalled because `97` and `98` define the rules and are unratified.

**Onboarding (66 §§12-17, 75):** `--situation` and `--label` are hard-required for the whole disk before a file is opened (66:461 CONTRADICTED; R-23, R-88 open) though per-branch situation (R-37) exists; Find is absent (66 §22 puts it first); `75` C1-C4 blocked on Q1, Q3, Q4; CONTEXTUAL answers never constructed (R-43); user-created templates and custom folders absent at the CLI (R-41's other halves).

**Wave 3, dispatched 11 Sep 11:45-12:10 local (no agent may fan out; model named on every dispatch):**
- SF-3 (Opus, `sf3-draft-groups`, dispatched 11:30, stalled on a stream watchdog at 12:05 and resumed): a group stays a draft until B's ratified verdict or a person's gesture accepts it; `approve_plan` is the person's gesture; the scoreboard passes its acceptance explicitly and the scorecard says so.
- `placement-judge` (Opus): the two-homes file goes to site C with both homes as candidates (ASK when it abstains); members judged inside the group's node; `missing_fields` and scores in the dossier behind the row-flag mechanism (R-77's), with the C-text sentence owed to the owner.
- `input-wiring` (Sonnet): the metadata discount handed its catalogue at both sites; the tar family through `tarfile`, by bytes; sensitive groups and the protected residual class derived from P7's flag; the template's own `max_depth` read.
- Queued behind SF-3 (same `cli.py` region): the tree-design surface (top-level candidates presented with their cards and approved before lower levels are designed; node types; the flatten rule fed a real measure; the nesting choice before freeze) and the residual surface (R-42's set gestures, per-set cards, user-defined areas, template slots read).

**Owner-only additions from the audit:** ratify or strike `97` (version lineage) and `98` (near-duplicate metric); `75` Q1, Q3, Q4 (household); R-88 (one situation per disk versus per branch); model-generated custom templates (`00`:97) after E acts; the C-text sentence for `missing_fields` and scores; the two empty exclusion categories (auto-save folders, previews: the comment says a name is added only when a real directory carries it -- the owner's tools decide).

**The ten, most severe first (the auditor's ranking, the lead's one-line each):** (1) Find is in no release -- no search command, no full-text index; (2) the engine takes the person's turn in tree design (candidates filtered by an answer supplied in advance, every directory adopted, the first nesting option taken, frozen in the same call); (3) the conflict oracle is wired to an empty tuple at `cli.py` ~13918, so the stop rule for irreconcilable facts and the dossier's `conflicts` list never fire -- sent to `placement-judge` as its item 4; (4) an unreviewed group is recorded accepted and coherent by rules (SF-3, building); (5) one whole-disk `--situation` and `--label` demanded before a file is opened (R-23, R-88); (6) every packet member lands at the packet's folder (`placement-judge` item 2); (7) exactly one schema per file at three sites; (8) two privacy sentences in the person's direction; (9) second volumes and name collisions stop files permanently (`apply_run/run.py` passes no cross-volume disposition; `cli.py` ~18180 hard-codes stop-and-ask with no gesture to answer it); (10) version families and near-duplicates never form (`version_family` uncalled, `near_match=lambda: False`, no perceptual hash carrier; the rules are `97` and `98`, unratified).

The pin that asserted the carve-out on purpose ("if this ever flips, the choice has to be remade") is re-argued: the owner remade the choice; no mode lets protected material out (29 passed).

**Four rulings, asked with a recommendation each; the owner's word:**
- **Find goes in Release 1, after the placement gate** ("Release 1, after the placement gate"): local, read-only, reads P11's retrieval (no second ranker), the six result states of `66` §3, the protected rule extended to search (`66` §4), five distinct no-result messages (`66` §5). One Opus agent after r24 is judged.
- **`--situation` becomes optional; each branch's situation comes from site G's evidence** ("Yes, optional, per branch from evidence"): the first run asks nothing; R-37's per-branch situation is the carrier; a typed `--situation` still overrides; the scoreboard keeps passing it. Closes R-88's contradiction and half of R-23.
- **Several schemas per file when the evidence supports them** ("Several schemas when evidence supports them"): the activation signals widen; a file carries every schema its evidence makes plausible; placement still picks one home (the owner's rule of 11 Sep 09:00, §18.36); links carry the rest.
- **`97` (version lineage) and `98` (near-duplicate metric) ratified as written** ("Ratify both as written"): `version_family` is wired under `97`'s rule with a blocking step that keeps the comparison sub-quadratic; the image reader supplies the perceptual hash `98` names and `near_match` is `98`'s metric at `98`'s threshold; the test that passes against a fixture reader while the deployment produces no carriers is re-argued against the real reader.

**Sequencing under the load:** the machine is at load 100-127 from three agents' test runs and r23b is starving (149 of 215, four hangs). No further agent is dispatched until the three report; then, one agent per area, model named, no fan-out: the tree-design surface (item 2 with R-92 and the flatten rule), the residual surface (R-42), the optional situation (R-88), multi-schema activation, `97`/`98`, and Find after r24.

**Finding 1, a two-homes file is the judge's question.** `run_corpus`'s multi-home loop asks site C first (`_two_homes_judged_steps` → `_asked_between_steps`): the file's own retrieval restricted to the two competing homes, a suppressed home added back carrying the group ids that pulled it, node-local graphs, `assess` over the two. Pins: essay to Essays and checklist to Forms by the judge's verdict; the ledger measured (the group's request carries `GROUP_BUDGET_SUFFIX`, no member's does, none deferred for cost).
**Finding 3, missing fields and scores.** `EvidenceItem` gains `missing_fields`, `score`, `margin`; filled from `assess`; written only when filled; gated by `PromptDefinition.lists_candidate_scores` (R-77's mechanism); the release door is `llm_harness/released_content.py`, not `src/privacy/` -- its named residual grows three to four (`missing_fields` joins `location`, `kind`, `conflicts[].kind`), for the owner's eye. r37 byte-identical. **Deliberate divergence:** `missing_fields` is the requirement sentence (`field for field in entry.expected_values if field not in fact_fields`), not `_levels_not_filled`, which measures what a shallow decision left unfilled and cannot run per candidate; one expression if the owner meant the other. **The owed C sentence is written as `c_placement.unratified.eliminate-v2r-group-levels-scores.2026-09-11` (30fe642)**, unratified; held by a strict xfail until ratified.
**Item 4, the conflict oracle: premise inverted.** Site C's `conflicts` list is not empty: `index.reachable_entries` walks the chain through the canonicaliser (gap 16), `retrieve` records one `ConflictConsidered` per stated value, `to_p8_conflicts` addresses them -- measured end to end for the first time by a new pin that passes as written. What is dead is P9's file-versus-file oracle at `cli.py` ~13918 (its three seams: `Group.conflicts`/`Membership.conflicts`, site B's dossier conflicts, SR4). **Owner-owed:** `00`:63 scopes SR4 to "irreconcilable course, institution, project, term, or purpose facts", five words that are neither field keys nor role refs; it cannot be widened to every field (`00`:112's own packet mixes an essay, a checklist, a screenshot and a transcript). Strict xfail carries the unblock: name the role refs, resolve through `role_bindings` as `group_level_fields_for` does, one `Conflict` per field with two canonical values among the anchor states.
**Also owed:** the shared branch as a third candidate on the judge's list (an owner question; "Shared Application Materials" is a real answer to "which home"); the two-homes record still carries `_flat_two_condition`'s figures (threading `assess`'s real margin would let a high-support two-homes file become auto-eligible -- not taken on the agent's judgement).

(folder names, never paths); "Tree health:" with "{group} -- {n}% of it is in this tree" and a sentence naming what is not measured; node marks `yours already`, `proposed`, `protected`, `ignored`, and one new phrase, `you made this one`; the nesting question's `unlocks`: "This decides the folders inside {branch}. No folder is built inside it until you answer, so the files that would go there wait with it." **Owner-owed:** the flatten rule stays `lambda: True` behind a strict xfail naming `BranchCounts` as the only carrier (counts of children and members are not retrieval value; the library's two `retrieval_*` fields are prose); `refinement_for`'s docstring promises an adopted folder gains levels from its own files, which the code cannot do (`horizontal_candidates` gives every existing-folder candidate no groups, so `opt_no_split` is its only option); the scan root prints as a card though it is never adopted; a folder with no immediate files prints as a 0-file area. The lead's parser fix: the per-branch situation pins slice the tree by indentation and now end at "Tree health:" and drop the node-type mark.

**SF-3's untyped pins.** The tree agent found 4 + 16 pre-existing failures the SF-3 chain had not covered: `tests/p15` (four files) and `tests/test_cli_the_screen_has_an_order.py` run the command without the acceptance and land on the proposal screen. Twenty pins; one Sonnet agent gives each the acceptance where its subject is downstream of it, or re-argues it where its subject is the proposal (dispatched 18:25).

### 18.51 The owner reads the second corpus's sheet: two findings (11 Sep 19:10 local)

**1. "Why are some not properly extracted?"** Measured on the 371-row sheet: 73 rows carry under 40 characters of extracted text, by type 28 jpg, 11 mp3, 9 png, 6 csv, 4 zip, 3 jpeg, 2 mis-spelt notebooks, 1 docx; 121 rows' first text unit is a metadata token with no space in it (a producer name, a format word) because the first unit the scan records is the METADATA unit, and the sheet's "what it says" column takes the first three units in row order -- a presentation defect of the lead's sheet, not of extraction, fixed by taking the first BODY or HEADING unit first. Real extraction gaps: photos whose OCR found no words (a photo of a scene has none; the image reader's properties-only rule means no caption model), mp3 (unsupported by design, R-33), 28 csv and 10 pdf runs `capped` (the per-scan ceiling, gap 22's values still the owner's), two `DuplicateUnit` refusals (an archive entry and a region claiming one container path -- a P4 keying defect, opened as **R-178**), one malformed zip, one psd (no reader). **R-178 (P, Medium, code):** `text_units` is keyed by `(run_id, container_path)` and an archive member's entry and its own first region collide; the second unit is refused rather than lost, correctly, but the file then reads as failed. Fix: the region's container path carries the entry it sits in.

**2. "The flu vaccine document was a Georgetown Prep document; it should be part of school. When I see it I understand, so something is wrong with our system."** Right, and it names the layer the design has and the build lacks: the person's OWN context. The document is a school health form; a person who knows they attend that school files it under the school, and the product cannot know that from the file alone -- the text says "vaccine", the vocabulary says health, and nothing says "this school is mine". `66` §§12-17 (onboarding) and `75` are exactly this: structural questions asked when a decision is blocked ("what is your relationship to this institution?"), role declarations (`--describe-role`, `--declare-role`, R-36's matcher), and the household and relationship categories (`75` C1-C4, blocked on Q1, Q3, Q4). What is built: the question registry, the reading-question flow, the role declaration gestures, the branch and home answers. What is owed: R-36's matcher prompt text (the owner's), `75`'s four questions (the owner's), and the CONTEXTUAL answer producer (R-43). **The owner's standing instruction, recorded:** the product will ask the person about their roles and more specific questions so the filing is fully customised; a file's situation is judged with the person's declared roles beside the evidence. This is the next Phase 5 build after Find, and the vaccine record is its first test case: with "student at this school" declared, the health form's home is the school's records, protected.

### 18.52 The owner's stop order: extraction and the database first (11 Sep 19:25 local)

**The order.** Reading the second corpus's sheet, the owner found four designed graphics (a club's donation-drive posts) filed as media with nothing extracted, though the words are on the image: "this is what we want to avoid. Stop all the work we have now. We need to first make sure the extraction and the database is perfect. Pause all phase work and perfect this first." Applied: r24, the corpus2 judged run, the bakeoff, Find and the onboarding build are halted; the gate suite on 16756dc runs on as verification of what is merged.

**The defect, found in twenty minutes.** The four images got `filesystem.record` and `image.metadata` runs and no OCR; across corpus2, OCR ran on 17 of 63 images. `extractors/ocr_policy.image_ocr_decision` runs OCR only when an image has no usable text and no usable metadata (§2.7), and "usable metadata" was any `zone=metadata` observation at signal tier 1 or 2 -- and `pixel dimensions` carried tier 2 whenever `dimension_signal` read the size as sensor-shaped, which it did for 1080x1080 (a design tool's square). So every image with a size counted as having usable metadata and OCR was skipped; the predicate's own docstring (2026-08-21) had fixed the same shape for `format` and dimensions at tier 3 and said §2.7's main path must not be dead. **Fixed (0b1f92c):** for the OCR decision an image's dimensions are never usable metadata, whatever their tier; camera EXIF, capture time and GPS still hold OCR back; the pin that asserted dimensions alone hold it back is re-argued as a strict xfail. **Owner-owed, already marked NEEDS JOSEPH in `extractors/image.py`:** the tiebreak between "sensor-shaped dimensions" and "exact display resolution" -- 1080x1080 is not a sensor shape.

**The extraction review that follows (the work now in front of everything):** corpus2 rescanned on the fixed head; then per file, the reader, completeness, units, characters and OCR words, judged per type as a person would ("would I say this file was read?"); every miss is a row here with a priced fix. Known so far: R-178 (an archive entry and its first region collide on one key), 28 csv and 10 pdf runs capped at gap 22's unset ceilings, mp3 unsupported by design, one psd with no reader, one malformed zip, and the sheet's own presentation defect (metadata token first), fixed.

### 18.53 The answer key and the extraction grade (11 Sep 19:50-20:20 local)

**The owner's order, restated at 20:00:** parse the files, get an accurate description of each and how the database should file it (the answer key), run our extractor against it, see the difference, fix, and say why. Done in that order; the classifier half waits on one decision (below).

**First correction.** The corpus2 scan consulted no model, so the product has filed nothing: zero placement decisions, tree nodes or groups in its database. The "draft situation / destination" the owner corrected on the sheet were the lead's own name-and-text rules, not the product. Grading the product's classifier needs a judged run on corpus2; that run is halted by the stop order and is the owner's call (recommendation below).

**Method.** A second, independent read of all 371 files with engines the product does not use: Apple PDFKit for PDF text (product: pdfminer/pdfium), Vision on every image and on PDF pages with under twenty characters (product: Vision under §2.7/§2.2 policy), `textutil` for docx/doc/odt (product: python-docx, Cocoa), stdlib for the rest, listing only for archives, no reader for mp3/psd/indd/8xp/acsm/crdownload. Then, per file, our characters against the second read's and the overlap of their word sets, sorted worst first; every file with an overlap under a half and every capped/failed/unsupported run was read by the lead by eye, side by side. Then the lead read every file's second-read head and wrote the answer key: what it is, the folder a person in the owner's position would file it under, the situation, protected or not, and a confidence. 371 entries; top-level folders School 90, Research 83, Community 58, Media 51, Career 46, Personal 33, Applications 6, Unsorted 4; 65 protected; confidence high 304, medium 44, low 23. The key is in the owner's private corpus folder beside the sheet (never in the repo); the sheet for the owner's corrections is a new file so the earlier sheet's edits are untouched.

**Grade before the fixes (scan2, the head with the dimensions fix only).** Per type, files where the second read had over 100 characters and ours under a quarter of it: jpg 6 of 9 (camera photos of a book page, 11,105 characters; of a memoir passage; of an insurance card -- all skipped because EXIF said "photograph"), docx 2 of 54 (flyers built from text boxes read as their filenames; a third at 33 characters), ics 3 of 4 (two failed whole on R-178, one carried only its DESCRIPTION), csv 21 of 51 (the 2,000-cell cap on datasets of up to fourteen million cells -- not a defect for filing: the head of a dataset says what it is), pdf 1 of 126 (a homework set with a typed cover and thirteen photographed pages; a scanned volunteer file with a scanner's garbage layer), xlsx 1 of 3 (cap). Median overlap: pdf 0.98, docx 0.99, doc 1.00, ipynb 0.99, txt 1.00, png 1.00, csv 0.86, jpg 0.00. A résumé came out at twenty times its length (169 copies of one merged cell).

**Five defects fixed and pinned (913f647), with the cause of each:**
1. *Every image is read* (`00` amendment 6). Cause: §2.7's trigger let any tier-1/2 metadata hold OCR back, so a photograph of a page was never looked at. `ocr_policy.image_ocr_decision` now runs OCR unless the run already stored text; three pins re-argued.
2. *A document is read page by page.* Cause: §2.2's "no text layer" was judged per file; a typed cover made thirteen scanned pages "a text layer", and P6 found a date on the cover and called it usable. `ocr_policy.sparse_pages` names the pages with no words (or under the deployment's floor, `cli.OCR_SPARSE_PAGE_WORDS = 20`, SPEC OQ1's deferred configuration value -- the policy module still holds no number); `dispatch._ocr` carries them in the run's config; `readers/ocr_vision.py` renders only those pages. Measured: 88 of 130 PDFs have pages under three words.
3. *Text boxes are read.* Cause: `Paragraph.text` reads a paragraph's own runs and a flyer's words sit in `w:txbxContent` under a drawing; Word writes each box twice (`mc:Choice` and `mc:Fallback`). `_box_paragraphs` reads the Choice copy, in order, each box paragraph taking an ordinal of its own; three identical boxes stay three.
4. *A merged cell is read once, and by identity.* Cause: python-docx returns a spanned cell once per grid column. The first fix used `id(cell._tc)` and lxml recycles a freed proxy's address, so a résumé's twenty-three distinct cells collapsed to 300 of 3,124 characters; `_read_table` now holds the proxies and compares with `is`, recurses into nested tables and reads boxes inside cells. Verified on the five files: characters equal to the XML's text runs.
5. *Entries sharing an identifier are told apart* (R-178, closed). Cause: an `entry` is label-addressed and a recurring event's exceptions share its UID. `_unique_label` keeps the format's identifier and disambiguates by RECURRENCE-ID, then by position; a second DESCRIPTION or NOTE in one entry is a second region. Both calendars now read (1,118 and 830 characters).

**Not defects, recorded so nobody chases them:** the csv "scramble" was the lead's dump ordering units by locator string; `text_units` stores a whole-file unit beside the per-region units for docx, doc, notebooks and text (storage doubles; the model's dossier carries one span-less body observation, so what the model reads is not doubled); the 50-page and 2,000-cell caps lose nothing a filing decision needs.

**Still open, priced:** (a) R-50's worker wedge recurred on the rescan of the fixed head -- the second camera photo held its worker ten minutes until the ceiling fired, the retry read it in seconds, and the files behind it waited; count per scan to be read from the finished rescan, and the wedge itself is a PyObjC-in-worker deadlock nobody has reproduced on demand (a day). (b) Sixteen mp3 files are unsupported by design (no transcription is authorised, §2.9); one psd, one InDesign file, one calculator program, two e-book loan tokens, one incomplete download and two empty notebooks have no reader -- a person would say "not a document" of each, and the filename is what files them. (c) Four scanned health forms whose text layer is only a page id -- caught by defect 2's rule now; the independent reader missed them too (its own twenty-character rule), so their answer-key entries rest on the filename and the sibling forms (medium confidence). (d) The privacy classifier, graded against the key: 11 sensitive_personal and 56 personal_non_sensitive against 65 the lead marks protected; a homework formatting guide and a course notebook were called sensitive, a signed physical examination form was not, and the insurance-card photo carried no signal because it was never read -- to be re-graded on the rescan, since classification reads extraction.

**Rescan on the fixed head (`~/.graph-agent/lead/corpus2-scan3`, launched 20:07 local):** numbers to follow in §18.54 when it ends.

**Decisions for the owner, each with a recommendation:** (1) the sparse-page floor is twenty words -- RATIFIED by the owner at 20:50 ("ok lets do 20 first now") after the measurement: floor 1 sends 207 pages of 38 files to Vision and misses 64 files whose thin pages carry one to nineteen stray words; floor 5 sends 691 of 98; floor 20 sends 892 of 102 under the 20-page cap, about thirty minutes of Vision on this corpus; the key as written stands for now ("looks detailed enough") and no classification runs yet; (2) grade the classifier: launch the judged run on corpus2 once the rescan's numbers are in -- recommended on the cloud route for speed (protected files stay local by amendment 5), and the lead reads the result against the key; (3) the top-level folders the key uses (School, Research, Community, Career, Personal, Media, Applications) are the lead's reading of the owner's life from the files -- the owner corrects the sheet, and those corrections become the key.

### 18.54 The rescan on the fixed head, the stall at its cause, the gate suite swept (11 Sep 20:20-21:30 local)

**The stall (R-112 at its cause; fixed at afa3f4b).** The rescan on 913f647 stopped after 9 files, again after 13, each time for the pool's whole 600 s ceiling with every worker idle at the call queue and the parent in `future.result`. Three reproductions outside the pool (sequential; eight spawned children; eight children with the product's readers built and the CLI imported) read every image in under 30 s, so the lead reproduced it inside the product on a generated corpus (24 PNGs of words, 8 image-only PDFs, txt, csv; nothing private) and handed it to an Opus agent. The cause, with `sample` and `lsof` evidence: each worker's FIRST Vision call opens Metal's shader-compiler cache, `libCoreFSCache` picks a numbered slot by looking for a free one, two workers that look in the same instant pick the same number, one takes the exclusive `flock` and the other blocks inside `MTLCompilerFSCache::openSync` with no timeout for as long as the winner lives; the winner then goes idle holding it, and a pool that takes results in submission order stops on the loser. 913f647 made it constant: with every image and every sparse page sent to OCR, all workers make their first call within the pool's first second. **Fix:** one process at a time makes its first Vision call (an exclusive `flock` on a per-user lock file with the cache's own reach); later calls in that process pass through. Measured on the generated corpus: 7 of 10 runs stalled without it, 0 of 7 with it. The agent's draft bounded the wait at sixty seconds; the lead removed the number: the kernel releases a `flock` when its holder dies, and a holder wedged in its own first call is killed by the pool's ceiling, the one bound this product puts on an extraction. Two pins in `tests/readers/test_ocr_vision.py`. The pool was behaving as designed and is untouched.

**The rescan (corpus2-scan4, afa3f4b):** all 371 files extracted in under two minutes, 105 OCR runs complete. Graded against the independent second read, per type, files where the second read has over 100 characters and ours under a quarter: pdf 0 of 126 (was 1), docx 0 of 54 (was 2), jpg 0 of 9 (was 6), ics 1 of 4 (was 3; the one left keeps its event titles as values, not text), csv 21 of 51 and xlsx 1 of 3 (the cell cap on datasets of up to fourteen million cells, by design), everything else 0. Median word overlap: pdf 0.97, docx 0.99, doc 1.00, jpg 0.99, png 1.00, ipynb 0.99, txt 1.00. The extraction half of the owner's order is met on this corpus.

**The privacy grade, and what it exposed.** The product marks 12 files protected against the key's 65: it caught 9, missed 56, three false alarms. The misses are not a detector defect: 304 of 371 files carry NO classification row, because the scans the lead ran passed no `--semantic-model` (the encoder's weights are on this machine and the flag was never given) and no local model, and the design lets only a local model classify an unclassified file (the per-file `G_SITUATION_SENSITIVITY` site is a LOCAL call). So a model-less scan cannot be graded for protection, and the lead's earlier scans were run one flag short. Consequence for the next step: no cloud model may be given a file until the local classification has run; the parsing run below is ordered accordingly.

**The gate suite** on 16756dc had 30 failures at `maxfail`; a Sonnet agent traced all 30 to nine deliberate 11 Sep changes whose pins were not swept plus one missed registration (`facts/lineage.py` absent from the P6 guard lists), none an ordering flake; swept and committed at 6dda464 (485 tests on the touched files, no assertion weakened; the report-at-scale screen budget re-measured from 120 to 180 lines with the residual card's two lines per set, owner-owed). The suite on afa3f4b is running; its result is appended below.

**The owner's rulings of 20:50-21:00:** the sparse-page floor is twenty; the answer key stands as written for now; no classification yet; the model belongs in parsing ("detailed info, DeepSeek for ordinary files, local for protected, think about privacy"); Sonnet and Opus agents for the work; "don't stop until everything is perfect and not hardcoded"; keep to the product design.

**The parsing run, designed (next).** An Opus agent is adding `--stop-after facts` so a run does everything through the fact pass (site A) and the three scan reports, then stops with nothing grouped or placed. The run: `--semantic-model ~/.graph-agent/models/minilm`, the local model for site G (every file, locally, for the safety domains) and for site A on protected files, the cloud route for site A on ordinary files, on the fixed head. Measured local latency on this machine under load: qwen3:8b reads 1,767 prompt tokens in 12.5 s and writes 80 tokens in 6.9 s, about 25 s a call, so site G over 371 files is on the order of two hours. Then two grades against the key: protection (65 protected) and the facts (`file_type` and the domain fields against the key's description and situation). Classification (sites B, C) stays halted by the owner's word.

### 18.55 The parsing run: a second spin at its cause, and the model phase measured (11 Sep 21:37-22:45 local)

**The gate suite on afa3f4b:** 10,407 passed, 3 failed, all three pins (the seven scopes; an unread-formats screen check that never accepted the proposal, the SF-3 shape the earlier sweep missed only because the suite stopped at thirty; a byte-identical golden that embedded its capture day's date and broke after midnight), swept and committed with `--stop-after facts` (58734b6; `--record` refused beside it too).

**The parsing run** (`--semantic-model`, the local model for the safety read and for protected files, `--enable-cloud` so site A may send ordinary files to DeepSeek, `--stop-after facts`) extracted all 371 files in under two minutes, classified 79 by the term detector and the encoder, and then sat at full CPU for forty minutes with the embedding count flat at 81 and no model call made. The model-less rescan of §18.54 did the same thing silently and took 71 minutes end to end. Three reproductions outside the run were clean (the embedding text builder on that file: 0.1 s; a generated four-megabyte export through the whole CLI: 100 s), so the lead gave the CLI a way to be asked: `faulthandler` on SIGUSR1, because on this platform neither `py-spy` nor `lldb` may attach to a busy process without root. The signal named it: `facts/rules.apply_rules` -> `write_unresolved(cache_key=pass_cache_key(...))` -> `_version_parts` -> `observations_for_file`. **The cause:** the pass cache key was derived by reading EVERY observation of the version (JSON, one row at a time) and asking P4 for each one's tier, and it is asked once per fact and once per refusal a pass writes; a four-megabyte data export carries 7,578 observations, thousands of them matching a rule whose context check fails, so the rules pass read the version's evidence thousands of times over -- observations squared. **Fixed (fb677ff):** one indexed join gives the two sets the key is made of, the row-by-row derivation stays as the oracle in the pin and as the path that names an observation whose run is missing; three pins. Measured: the relaunched run passed the point of the spin in under a minute and had 212 files classified six minutes later.

**The model phase, measured.** The situation site (G, local by design) began at 22:36: qwen3:8b, dossiers of 6,492 to 8,607 prompt tokens, 47 to 137 completion tokens, one call every 33 to 42 seconds. At that pace the site over 371 files is on the order of four hours, before site A on protected files. The owner has ruled local too slow; the levers, priced, for the owner: (a) a smaller local model for the safety read (qwen2.5:3b is on this machine, about twice the speed, a quality question the bakeoff answers); (b) a tighter dossier for site G (the bound is 4,000 tokens and the prompt itself is the rest; the template's length is the owner's to ratify); (c) nothing on the cloud, by amendment 5. The run is left to finish tonight as the measured baseline; the two grades (protection against the key's 65; `file_type` and the domain fields against the key's description and situation) follow in §18.56.

### 18.56 The parsing run's two deaths at their cause, site G graded against the key, and the protection grade (12 Sep 12:10-14:00 local)

**Both deaths were the lead's own signal.** The `SIGUSR1` stack-dump registration added in fb677ff used `chain=True`, which re-raises to the handler it replaced; the handler it replaced was the default one, whose action for that signal is to end the process. The overnight run (relaunch 2) was asked where it was at 12:12 and died of the question, exit 158 = 128 + the signal's number; run 1 ended the same way. Fixed in c16ad77: `cli.say_where_you_are_when_asked`, `chain=False`, pinned by `tests/test_cli_says_where_it_is.py`, whose child receives the real signal, prints its stacks and exits 0.

**The nine-hour stall was a second quadratic.** The dump before the death showed `facts/anchor_statements._containing_span_reading`: for every code reading it asked `observation_key` (a sha256 over locator and words, computed on every access) of every sibling, so one sheet whose 14,607 cells all print a course code cost 14,607 × 14,607 × 2 digests. c16ad77 adds `_span_index`: each reading keyed once per file version, grouped by container path, narrowest first, so a code's line is the first containing entry of its own container; same answer as the scan (pinned by the page-over-heading-over-identifier case and by a count of key computations on a 300-reading sheet: under ten per reading against the former three hundred). 77 targeted tests pass.

**Relaunch 3** (12:16 local, `python3 -u`, same database) reached site G by 13:45. Site G answers one file every 33 to 42 seconds on qwen3:8b; the run should end in three to four hours. Nothing has reached the cloud. Grades of the fact pass follow when it ends.

**Site G graded against the answer key, 87 dossiers so far.** The key's situations are the lead's own vocabulary; for grading each is mapped to the library schema whose rows hold it (coursework, online courses, tests, records and programmes to `academic`; papers, datasets and reading to `research`; clubs to `nonprofit`; media to `photos`/`creative`; health to `medical`; immigration and accounts to `identity`; travel to `finance`/`photos`; the application packet to `college_applications`).
- **The menu.** Site G's `allowed_vocabulary` is the recognisers' shortlist. The key's schema is on it for 35 of 87 files; menus hold one candidate plus `none_of_these` for 28 files, and the members offered most are `construction_property` (27) and `finance` (25) on a student's Downloads folder. The scan's typed `--situation` is never on the menu.
- **The answers.** 60 of 83 verdicts abstain (`none`): 40 where the answer was not on the menu, 20 where it was. 21 accepted: 12 right, 8 wrong (a precalculus exercise sheet called `research`; club records called `academic` and `legal`; an online-course file called `construction_property`; a dataset called `medical`; reading-library files called `creative` and `finance`), 1 that named a protected kind on a file whose situation is off the map (right on the protection axis). 2 rejected on `CITATION_SPAN_MISMATCH`, one of them a correct `boarding_pass_or_ticket` verdict on an e-ticket whose citation was retyped rather than copied: the file stays local, so the direction is safe, and the exact-copy rule is recorded here as the thing that threw a right answer away.

**Protection graded against the key, current classification rows (no `superseded_by`).** Key protected 65: product protected 20, missed 45. Key ordinary 306: product ordinary 290, over-protected 10 (6 by the rules' `safety_domain`, 4 by site G).
- **32 of the 45 misses are the vocabulary question already owed to the owner**, not a mechanism: résumés (14), club sign-up and roster records with other people's details (9), application essays (4), report cards (5). The design classes these `personal_non_sensitive`; the key marks them protected. The owner rules.
- **13 misses fall inside the design's own four domains**: health records and forms (7), immigration papers (2), a credential (1), a medical letter inside an application packet (1), an exam admission ticket (1), a travel record (1). Of these, 4 carry basis `detector` with `protected = 0` -- the rules RECOGNISED an ordinary schema on a health authorisation form and a state health department form whose OCR text names the department -- so they are cloud-eligible right now; 5 carry `detector_no_safety_evidence` (local only); 3 have no classification row at all (local only); 1 is site G's own miss (an immigration paper called `college_applications`).
- **43 of the 45 were never put to any model.** Site G asks only files the rules could not settle or are holding (`00`:110 as §17.1 reads it, gap 24b); a file the rules settle as ordinary goes to the cloud on the rules' word alone, and the rules' own measured top-1 accuracy on this corpus is 32.2% (`cli.SEMANTIC_MAX_ANCHOR_WORDS`'s comment). 218 files carry a rule classification; 153 carry none (the rules abstained, the vector abstained, no hold): those stay local, and 57 of the 85 files site G has asked are among them.

**What this measures, in the constitution's words.** The model is the judge, and at the one site that decides whether a file may leave the device it is asked about a minority of files, from a menu that omits the answer three times in five, and a health form the rules called ordinary is released without the question ever being put. The fix is not more authored words -- `00` §3 says fixed patterns cannot capture "unlabeled forms", and the owner forbids hardcoding -- it is putting every file to the local model at site G before any cloud release, with the whole library as the menu and the recognisers' candidates carried as evidence rather than as the cage. **This is the owner's ruling, not the lead's**, because it touches three recorded lines: `00`:39's stage two ("identify obvious candidate domains" -- kept, as evidence items), `00`:110 read onto site G by §17.1 ("not called for direct, unique matches"), and the ratified template's "the kinds it came nearest to", which would need a new row. Costs, measured: about 1,200 more prompt tokens per dossier for 23 candidate descriptions (dossiers today run 2,400 to 8,600 under a 4,000 bound, so the bound would need to give way or the descriptions shorten); 371 files × 35-45 s ≈ 3.6-4.6 h per corpus on qwen3:8b, once per content hash, or the qwen2.5:3b lever. Pins re-argued: `SituationQuestion`'s "recognised without a hold is refused", `shortlist_for`'s list-of-one refusal, gap 24b's settled count. Until the ruling the run stands as the "before".

**Library gaps the key exposes**, for the owner: the library has no row for a student club's own records (62 files here), personal media outside a camera or messenger export (51), online learning outside a school (24), a person's own health records outside `medical.personal-health-records`' wording (15), or personal travel (9). Whether these are new rows or the key's vocabulary is wrong is the owner's.

**Not yet:** the facts grade (`file_facts` per file against the key's `what`), when the run ends; the full gate suite on c16ad77, alone, after the run (swap is at five of six gigabytes with the model resident).

**The owner's rulings of 14:20 local, applied.** (1) *Résumés, club rosters and records with other people's details, application essays and forms, and report cards are ORDINARY.* The design's `personal_non_sensitive` stands; the answer key is corrected (36 entries flipped, each carrying the ruling; key protected is now 29: health records 15, travel 9, immigration 2, household 1, accounts 1, an exam admission ticket 1). Protection regraded on the corrected key: protected caught 17, missed 12 (basis `detector` 4 -- cloud-eligible on the rules' word -- `detector_no_safety_evidence` 5, no row 2, site G 1); ordinary right 323, over-protected 13 (rules' `safety_domain` 9, site G 4). (2) *The small items are the lead's.* Done in this commit: the local client LOADS the model once, with a promptless generate request under the whole-call patience, before the first file's clock is armed (`readers/model_ollama.GENERATE_PATH`, `load_once`; every launch's first call and every call after an eviction had been lost to `OllamaRanOutOfTimeWaitingForAToken` with the load still running -- 7 failures in this run, 5 of them one stall under swap at 18:04-18:10 UTC); the citation check folds whitespace on both sides and nothing else, so a heading quoted across a line break is present (`llm_harness/validation._folded`; the e-ticket verdict above). Library rows: `academic.online-course`, `medical.personal-health-records`, `travel.bookings-confirmations` and `travel.trip-photos` already exist, so three of the five gaps were the key's vocabulary, not the library's; a student club's own records and personal media outside a camera or messenger export remain without a row and wait on the menu ruling, which decides whether rows or the model carry them. (3) *"Should we run completely cloud?"* -- answered in session: everything ordinary already goes to the cloud; the local model exists only to decide whether a file may leave the machine and to read the files that may not; the lead recommends keeping only that gate local, on the smaller model, and measuring it against the larger one's verdicts when this run ends.

### 18.57 Amendment 7 built and run: the layered gate, the whole-library situation, the cloud for a cleared file (12 Sep 15:50-late)

**The owner's go (16:05): "we wanna do this. ok full scale run and build this right now."** Built by four agents in isolated worktrees on a clean baseline (8e8c9bf: 10,427 passed) and merged in order: library rows (3a1625f), the entity reader (e4da2d5, ecccbd7), identifier patterns (ae58177), entity holds (b8ed017), the site G split (a561adc), then the lead's privacy patch and routing (3897e55). Prompt rows authored by the lead and ratified by the owner at 17:15 (d3a49d1): the gate `ratified_local`, the whole-library situation `ratified`; an example-free twin kept as the reserved A/B candidate.

**What was built.**
- *7(a) identifier patterns* (`extractors/identifiers.py`): eight kinds on their schemes' own checksums (payment card, IBAN, bank account, US SSN, passport, HKID, date of birth beside a label, medical record number); the span names only the masked tail; readings ride on the host run; the detector holds a file on one as on an authored term. Three over-fires found in review and shut (random floats passing Luhn; `dob` inside `Adobe`; an invented window).
- *7(b) entity reader* (`readers/entities_gliner.py`, `extractors/entities.py`): GLiNER at full precision on the MiniLM seam, torch-free; the spike measured int8 collapsing calibration (recall 0.04) and fp16 failing to load on the CPU runtime, so full precision ships: 2.36 GB resident, 250 ms per 1,000 characters, precision 0.80 / recall 0.90 at floor 0.7. It windows the whole stored text (`ENTITY_CHAR_BUDGET` 20,000) and runs as its own phase, released before the local language model loads. Entities hold in the detector: a person beside a diagnosis is medical, beside an identity number or a date of birth is identity; an account or medical record number holds alone; "beside" is the same text unit. Because the entity pass runs after the per-file classify loop, `_reclassify_on_entities` asks the detector once more over the roster so the hold reaches the row.
- *7(c) the gate and the situation*: `H_restricted_kind`, a local site asking which of the ten restricted kinds a file is, over a 1,200-token dossier, for every file the layers did not hold; its clearance row (`local_model_gate`, protected 0) is what opens the cloud. Site G asks every file, from the whole library (24 candidate items, about 1,244 tokens), the recognisers' candidates carried as evidence; routed to the cloud for a cleared file (bound 2,750 tokens) and locally otherwise. A cloud route needs a local model's word or the person's (`CLOUD_CLEARING_BASES`); the rules' word alone keeps a file local. Pass order: entities → reclassify → gate → situation → facts.
- *Library rows*: `nonprofit.student-organisation`, `photos.personal-graphics`.

**The run (`corpus2-gate1`, 18:30 local, `--entity-model --semantic-model --enable-cloud --stop-after facts`), interim at 19:20:** 135 gate calls at about 15 seconds each (the old situation call took 35-45), 118 cleared, 82 held, 0 failures, 0 cloud sends before the gate finished. Protection at 18:50, before the gate reached the rest: caught 20 of 29 (17 before the build), the 9 not yet reached all carrying rules-only or no rows, so none cloud-eligible. Over-protection by the deterministic layers: 47 ordinary files, 20 of them an author's name beside a disease term in a paper's abstract, 7 club sign-ups carrying other people's dates of birth, 12 the authored terms as before. None is lost: a held file goes to the local situation judge, which may lift the hold; the cost is one local call each.

**Two defects the run found, and what they cost (19:48-21:20).** (1) Site G built its route through `target_for`'s default `operation_mode='offline'` rather than the run's, so every gate-cleared file went to the local model with the 4,000-token bound; fixed by threading the mode (6e4e412), the run relaunched on the same database at 19:48 (`scan.out.run1` is the first run). (2) The relaunch died eight situation calls in, on a correct answer: under 7(c) a file no recogniser raised anything for is asked from the whole library, its question carries no observation keys, and `situation_classification` built a `local_model_situation` record with nothing to cite -- `UnbackedClassification`, exit 1 (`scan.out.run2`). The record now cites what the verdict's citations resolved to beside what the recogniser raised (`cited_observations`, P8-checked), and is `None` -- counted as declined -- when neither has a key (45d36ca0). Before it died the relaunch had sent one gate-cleared file to the cloud at the 2,750 bound, so 7(c)'s cloud route is verified on the wire.

**The relaunch tax, and its removal.** Each relaunch had re-asked every gate question (~290 at 15 s, 75 minutes) and would have re-asked every situation question, because neither site recorded §18.31's call identity. Both now write site E's three rows -- identity, prior lookup, reuse -- through one shared `_per_file_call_identity`; the gate reuses a kind or a clearance (`gate_kind_named_before`), site G reuses an accepted verdict read back whole (`store.stored_verdict`, `situation_verdict_before`), and each pass counts `reused` outside its partition with its own sentence (45d36ca0, f1d57b08). Run 3 (21:17, `run3.sh`, same database) still pays the gate once, since run 2's verdicts carry no identity rows; every run after it does not. Nine scoreboard-reuse pins were stale from the gate merge (fail identically on 44d93f5); swept by an agent, recorded below.

**Runs 3 to 6 (12 Sep 21:17 to 13 Sep 03:00): what the gate could not see, four times.** Run 3 graded 22 of 29 protected caught. The seven misses had two causes, each measured on the stored dossiers rather than guessed: (1) the gate's and site G's purses were the observe ceiling of 200 calls, so the last 106 askable files of a 371-file roster were refused `BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` before any call on every run -- four health forms among them; both purses are now the roster (`gate_scan_budget`, `situation_scan_budget`, d505e901). (2) The opening excerpt was minted at the stored 4,000-token ceiling and never fitted the gate's 1,200, so the gate read six metadata envelopes and a page number and cleared the file on them; the excerpt is now sized by the call's own ceiling (`ordered_releasable_observations(ceiling=)`), path and metadata zones are released last (`GATE_ZONES_LAST`, `SITUATION_ZONES_LAST`), an OCR whole-page unit -- which carries a span and so was never excerpted -- is cut like any other opening (`opening_reading_for`), and the gate's bound is 3,000, spent as wire bytes with the excerpt at `ceiling // limit` characters (023b79e7, 5c008b41). Two more: the local silence deadline of 90 s killed four of seven local situation calls reading 7,100-token prompts under swap (now the whole-call 600 s), and the released keys joined the call identity so a wider release is a new question. Run 5, text-first, lifted protection to 26 of 29 before it was stopped at three quarters of the gate; the stored state graded 27 of 29 on 13 Sep 03:15, with 0 key-protected files sent to the cloud across every run (54 cleared files sent).

**The two that remain (03:15).** A two-factor code sheet and an entry/admission record. No deterministic layer holds either: the identity domain's authored work types ("backup codes", "recovery codes", "two-factor recovery-code sheet", "entry, admission, or arrival/departure record") do not appear word-for-word in either file, the design's own rule for a code grid beside a backup or recovery context (`identity.credentials-passwords`, R2 `det-credential-2fa-backup-codes`) was never implemented in `extractors/identifiers.py`, and the entity reader found nothing on either. The 8B gate, shown the text, named the first correctly in its own words and still answered `none_of_these` -- "not one of the ten kinds" -- with "credentials and password vaults" on the list in front of it. The close is vocabulary, and vocabulary is the owner's: five identity work types are proposed for ratification (two-step verification, two-step verification codes, form I-94, arrival/departure record, class of admission), applied on c4cae5d3's precedent (node row, recompile, ruling test) the moment the word is given. Measured before asking (04:25, the CLI's own detector on the corpus database, shipped manifest against the proposed one): the five terms newly hold exactly three files, all three key-protected -- the code sheet and two immigration records -- and no ordinary file; the shipped vocabulary recognises the code sheet as a manufacturing document.

**The 4B experiment (run 7, 03:03-03:28) is over.** `qwen3:4b` at the gate echoed the request envelope back -- call site, schema, policy -- as its whole answer on three of three calls (`SCHEMA_INVALID`, 2,200-3,300 completion tokens, two to three minutes each). Slower and empty; the lead's first speed lever is closed by measurement.

**`--stop-after gate` (32ab4fcd).** The one stage the flag offered was `facts`, and a run to the facts passes site G, whose cloud route the two files above would take. The stage joins the same tuple: the gate answers on this device, `_model_fact_pass` returns with its own not-run reason, the screen says where the run ended. Run 8 (03:33, `run8.sh`, `qwen3:8b`, same database) finishes the gate under it -- run 5's answers reused, the last quarter asked -- so protection is graded with zero releases while the two files wait on the owner's word; the situation and the facts run after it.

**Final grade (run 8, gate complete, 13 Sep 04:16).** Protection against the owner's corrected key: **28 of 29 protected files caught, 1 missed; 0 key-protected files sent to the cloud in any run; 54 cleared files sent** (all key-ordinary). The entry/admission record was caught the moment the gate read its text rather than its metadata. The one miss is the two-factor code sheet: named correctly by the gate in its own words and answered `none_of_these`; the close is the identity vocabulary above, owner's word owed. Over-protection: 102 ordinary files held (57 named by the gate -- medical record and financial statement lead -- and 45 by the rules: club sign-ups carrying dates of birth, papers with an author's name beside a disease term); every one goes to the local situation judge, which may lift the hold. Gate outcomes over 1,140 answers: 1,045 `none_of_these`, 87 a named kind accepted, 8 rejected -- the rejects are the 8B's own typos in a citation key on answers carrying 12 to 18 citations, three of them naming a kind correctly; a rejected answer writes no row, so an earlier clearance of the same file stands, which is a follow-up beside the gate-specific response cap. Reuse: 246 of the gate's answers on run 8 were read back from the database rather than asked. Database integrity: 371 files, 77,800 evidence rows, 0 empty, 0 classifications without evidence, 0 duplicate current rows, 0 verdicts without a dossier, 410 facts all with evidence. Site G and the facts run next (`--stop-after facts`), once the vocabulary is ruled on. **The owner's word (13 Sep 05:45) and the run it opened.** The five identity work types were ratified and applied (ab65b734; ruling test on the 11 Sep precedent). Simulating the relaunch on a copy of the corpus database found the hold would NOT have landed: `learning_seam._outranked_by` scored the gate's `llm_supported` clearance above the rules' `possible` hold, so the code sheet would have stayed cleared and gone to the cloud. Fixed before launch (9ab87f90): a protected deterministic record retires an unprotected gate clearance whatever the two reliability states say, because the clearance was written on the premise that the layers were silent. Run 9 (11:26, `run9.sh`, `--stop-after facts`, gate reused, local judge kept for the 130 held files) holds the code sheet as `safety_domain` before any model. Three follow-ups closed beside it (96e9da61): a gate row opens the cloud only for a file THIS run's gate cleared (`GatePass.cleared_files` asked by `model_route_permitted` at G and A), so a clearance from a question the run did not answer sends nothing; the local client's response cap is 4,096, measured against 1,208 as the longest accepted local answer; the entity pass skips a file version already carrying its readings, so a relaunch no longer spends twelve minutes re-reading an unchanged corpus.

Full suite on the merged head caa339a1: 10,573 passed, 0 failed, 20 skipped, 34 xfailed (17 min); the 27 pins stale since the 12 Sep merges were re-argued by an agent on c195cfed's precedent, and one of this session's own (the Word-document short-ceiling pin) around the excerpt's own bound.

### 18.58 The sort stage opened: what the audit found and what was built before the first sort run (13 Sep 12:00-13:00)

**The owner's order (11:20): run in parallel and move onto the next stage; it had better be perfectly sorted.** With run 9 (site G and the facts) on the machine, an Opus audit read P8-P11 against `00`'s amendments and the scoreboard's buckets and found four gaps, each of which would have made a sort wrong, spill, or place what should abstain:

1. **A configured model that gave no answer still placed the file** (`placement/pipeline.py` ~1955-2016): `CallRefused`, `PreCallAbstention` (including `BUDGET_EXHAUSTED`), `CallFailed`, `ValidationUnavailable` and R-136's unbuilt call skipped the verdict branch and the file was placed by rule as `decided_by=rule`. The rulings the code cited (R-O, R-136, R-173) are about the run not dying and say the fallback "is ruled when a measured run produces it"; no owner sentence sanctions it, and `00`'s "every placement goes through the model... whenever a model is configured" contradicts it. Fixed: such a file abstains as `no_model_judgement` (a new §6.10 reason word, its own review set), R-74's narrow arm kept, the no-model deterministic path unchanged (14016770).
2. **A group member the model never read inherited the branch**: `_group_evidence` carried only cited items, so a factless member was absent from the group dossier and `contradicts_the_group` could not fire. Fixed: it sits apart and is placed alone (7d57f1fb).
3. **Placement was situation-blind.** Site G names a situation per file and the fact pass asks under it, but the partition opened a branch only for an anchored schema and `src/placement/` read no situation at all, so a research paper in a coursework run was filed into a course. Fixed at three seams (59f099e4, f4857699): `PipelineInputs.situation_of` and `the_situation_each_branch_carries` (read off `_the_situation_its_folders_are_chosen_under`: G's name, else the file's branch, else the run's); `branch_situation.named_by_the_model` opens a branch for a schema G named, ahead of the anchor, and `cli` partitions again after the fact pass; step 6's `_only_this_files_own_branch` drops candidates (and the set-aside tail) under another situation's root, abstaining `no_supported_destination` when nothing survives; and the group path now sits a member apart when the group's answer is under another situation's branch (the lead, after the merge). The scorer reads ONE run against every label, bucketed by the label's situation, with `contaminated` redefined as "placed under a branch that is not this file's label's".
4. **A protected file could reach `outcome = place`** through R-74's arm. Fixed: never; it is held with the privacy reason at the one statement that builds a place (6f7c25ac).

Merged as 63bf807a and 6bd6e155; 819 pins in the sort stage's files pass together on the merged head. Left open, deliberately: the end-to-end hand-off (second partition -> placement inputs -> filter) has no single pin, because no harness drives a placing corpus through a stub local model; a branch G opened with no accepted group builds no folders, so its files abstain rather than move. The first sort run (run 10, `run10.sh`, same database, `--accept-groups`, no `--apply`) waits for run 9 to finish; it is scored with `score10.sh` on a copy.

### 18.59 The local judge's word, measured and withdrawn; the owner's rulings of 13 Sep (13:40-14:30)

**What run 9 showed at 13:42.** With site G asking the 130 held files on this device, the local judge (qwen3:8b, 92 s a file at the median, prompts of 8,600 tokens, a third of its answers rejected for a mangled citation key) was right 17 times, wrong 42 and silent 10 against the key -- and it LIFTED the holds on two key-protected files, a test-score record held by the rules and a travel document held by the gate. Under `CLOUD_CLEARING_BASES` as ratified on 12 Sep a lifted hold was a cloud clearance: both would have crossed at the fact pass. The run was stopped before it; neither file has a cloud usage row.

**Three changes, one purpose (ed4b7047):** the cloud opens to the gate's word or the person's, never the situation judge's; `ask_the_situation` writes no ordinary row over a standing hold; a situation row that lifted a hold before today is retired by the hold it lifted (`learning_seam._outranked_by`, the gate-clearance rule extended). Pinned on the synthetic corpus and simulated on a copy of the owner's: eight lifted rows return to their holds, one of them key-protected; the travel document's gate hold returns through the gate's reused answer.

**The owner's word (14:05), applied (0b396073).** (1) *Skip*: a protected record is filed by the person, so a held file is not put to the local judge at all -- the holds block says "held and not asked". Run 9's local half, about 3.5 hours, is gone. (2) *An author beside a disease term in a paper is not a patient record*: measured first -- in every key-protected record the unit naming a person beside a condition names ONE condition; in every research paper or dataset analysis held by that pair it names seven to thirteen -- so the pair holds nothing in a unit naming `TOPIC_CONDITION_MENTIONS` (5) or more; on the corpus it clears 11 held files, all ordinary, and loses none of the 29. (3) *A page of several people's dates of birth is a roster*: measured, and it has no deterministic handle -- each club sign-up carries ONE person's date of birth beside one name, exactly the shape of an identity document; the readers store a masked tail, so neither count nor value tells them apart. Reported to the owner; the lever left is judgement, and the gate never sees a rules-held file.

**Run 9 relaunched at 14:25** on the same database: the gate and the cloud's situation answers reused, no local situation call, then the facts. Review lists for the owner's manual check written beside the run (`review/`: the 29 protected by the key and whether each is held; the ordinary files held and what fired; the situation disagreements; the gate's named kinds) -- on the owner's machine, never in this document.

**The cloud judge and the questions stage against the design (Opus audit, 14:30-15:00; 95d7b833, f3d656b5).** Applied: the folder path is released FIRST to the situation judge (`zones_first`, the mirror of `zones_last`) -- measured on a synthetic corpus, a 2.4 KB body unit spent 2,725 of the cloud's 2,750 bytes and the fill cut the path, both metadata readings and the rest of the text, against the 9 Sep amendment ("whole units, folder paths and OCR text within that ceiling"); and two stale sentences at site G. Being built: one situation per file for a file the judge left silent (59 of 371) -- the fact pass took the branch vote, the folder chooser the branch's own situation, site E the run's, so a silent file was asked one situation's fields and refused its folders. Owner's, with the measured cost: (1) the menu has no honest member for a club -- the situations exist (`nonprofit.member-association`, `academic.online-course`, `photos.social-media-export`) but the menu is the 23 schemas and the ratified prompt tells the judge to find "a student organisation stated by a club, an event, a sign-up or a roster" with nothing to name it, which is the 47 wrongs; naming each schema's situations on its item costs 1,011 tokens per dossier, doubling the frame; a schema row costs nothing per call. (2) A schema the judge names resolves to its alphabetically first situation (`research` -> `conference-presentation`, `finance` -> `cap-table-equity`), against §11.2 step 4 -- matters at the sort stage.
