(3) A person's answers reach no learning record (`apply_answers` -> `structural_answers`, no event; `=skip`/`=revoke`/`not_listed` unreadable by `learning_records`), against 00:262-263; `SCOPE_ORGANIZATION` is a vocabulary member the owner adds. (4) No gesture files a held file: `privacy/moves.py` already permits a move when `automatic_move_permissions[file_id]` is true and nothing writes it -- one per-file gesture at the sort stage. (5) The prompt's line "There is no document, no page, no filename, no path, no rest of the file" is false of the path, which is released to every target; exact replacement proposed: "...no filename, no rest of the file. A folder path may be among them." (6) The person is asked the situation before the judge runs (`_partition_branches` raises `NotConfigured` before the fact pass) -- structural, the 11 Sep item 2 says a situation is not demanded of the person before a file is opened.

**Run 9 (fourth launch, 15:25-15:57): the situation and the facts, done.** Two defects of the day's own making found and closed before it: the cloud door narrowed at 13:50 read the current row's basis, and the judge's row supersedes the gate's, so every cleared file's facts went to the local model (10 files, 24 calls, 14 rejected, 90 s each, run stopped at 15:15) -- the door now asks the gate pass which files it cleared this run (8652a4c7, pinned on the wire with the judge answering every file); and the fact pass withholds a protected file on the same word that stopped site G asking one (its route stays local-only as ruled on 9 Sep; the pin that expected a local dossier is re-argued). Numbers against the key: **protection 29 of 29 caught, 0 key-protected files sent to the cloud in any run** (246 cleared files sent; the six files the product now holds that were sent earlier as cleared are all ordinary by the key); over-protection 101 (60 named by the gate, 33 by the rules, 8 by the judge naming a safety domain as a paper's situation); the cloud judge's latest answer per file right 142, wrong 51, silent 42 -- the wrongs led by club-event photos called "photos" (12), résumés and cover letters called "college_applications" (10), travel documents answered with a restricted kind instead of a situation (6) and papers on medical topics called "medical" (6); facts 472 (259 by rule, 179 by the family producers, 34 by the model) -- the cloud fact pass abstained on 474 field claims and accepted 13, saying "no course code", "only the filename" or "metadata", and a quarter of its dossiers carried 105 characters of text or fewer (the photos and scans). Coverage: 200 files asked, 130 protected and not asked, 6 refused by the gate before anything was sent (the always-local and protected-records templates), and **35 deferred by the fact pass's own 200-call ceiling** -- the third site to carry the observe ceiling past a 200-file roster; `fact_scan_budget` is now the roster with 200 as the floor, and the 35 are asked on the next launch. Database integrity clean (371 files, 77,915 evidence rows, 472 facts all with evidence, 8 call failures recorded). **Run 9's fifth launch (16:33-17:52), with the roster-sized purse: 0 deferred** -- 184 settled by rule, 48 asked, 8 refused by the gate before anything was sent, 131 protected and not asked; facts 485 on 188 files; 29 of 29 protected held; 0 key-protected files sent; integrity clean. The situation pass's counters partition the roster again (`held_not_asked`, 5997c720), which the pin sweep found broken by the skip.

### 18.60 The owner's review of 13 Sep (21:00): ordinary files must not be held; the layers measured and rebuilt (13 Sep, 21:00-)

**The owner's word, reading the review lists on their machine.** Every ordinary file in the "ordinary but held" and "gate-named kinds" lists "MUST NOT BE HELD"; the standardised-test score report the key marked protected is ordinary (the key is corrected; 28 protected remain); the facts column of the per-file list is "useless"; a held file must get a situation answer and facts; the cloud judge must reach 96% before the sort run; the club menu member: "add that"; the four §18.59 rulings are ratified as memory of the person. One list carries ten files the key marks protected (six health records, two immigration papers, a travel document, a household record) under the same "all ordinary" note; those are kept protected until the owner names them.

**The rules, measured and rebuilt (3b4ac747).** Before the change the rules held 33 ordinary files and 14 of the 29 protected. Every one of the 33 rested on ONE signal: eight club sign-up sheets on a date of birth beside a name (7(a)'s label pattern plus 7(b)'s pair), ten essays and personal statements on an author or a relative beside a condition word, four on the word `will` (a modal verb in body prose), the rest on one word in a table, a filename or a heading (`patient`, `vaccine`, `statement`, `order confirmation`). Every protected file the rules caught carried a word that says what the record is AND a second finding beside it. So: a work type in a naming zone holds alone (the 11 Sep pins stand); a work type in body prose holds only with corroboration -- a deterministic reading of the same domain, or a person the entity encoder named anywhere in the file (`Detector._corroborated`); a checksummed identifier (`extractors.identifiers.CHECKSUMMED_KINDS`) holds alone; a date of birth, a passport-shaped number and 7(b)'s pair corroborate and hold nothing by themselves; `will` leaves the legal work types. Measured with the real detector on the corpus: ordinary held 33 -> 4 (an `order confirmation` heading on a programme's page, a `statement` heading on a club's, a checksum-valid HKID in a reading-library file, `vaccination` beside a name on a dataset -- each reportable by name to the owner), protected held by the rules 14 -> 15 of 29 (eight travel documents on `credit card`/`endorsement` beside a passenger, six health records, one household). The 14 protected files the rules release are read next by the gate. Fifteen recognition pins encoded the pair-alone and body-term-alone holds and are re-argued (agent). Amendment 7(b)'s sentence "a person beside a diagnosis holds the file without a model call" is now false as written and goes to the owner with the numbers: ten of eleven such pairs on this corpus were sentences about a life, not records of a diagnosis.

**And the rules take a hold back (7f5deaa7).** A rules hold was written once and stood until something outranked it: a paper the 14:05 papers ruling released was still held at 21:00, because the detector writes nothing on an abstention. `_reclassify_on_entities` now retires a `safety_domain` row whose `precaution_report` is `None` under today's rules -- the recognition if there is one, else a `detector_no_safety_evidence` row over the hold's own observations. Neither opens the cloud (`CLOUD_CLEARING_BASES`); the file is read next by the gate.

**The gate's stale answers (3b4ac747, 7f5deaa7).** Of 320 gated files, 238 kept the answer given on a 1,200-byte dossier -- metadata and a page number -- after the ceiling went to 3,000 on 12 Sep, because the per-file call identity named WHICH readings were shown (`context_refs`) and not HOW MUCH of each: the excerpt producer cuts a unit's opening to `ceiling // limit` characters, so the same five keys under two ceilings were one question to the cache. `max_dossier_tokens` is the eleventh identity term (`llm_harness.store.CALL_IDENTITY_DIMENSIONS`, empty value `None`, so every prior per-file answer is asked once more under its true bound; sites A and E carry it too). The gate's reasons on the 38 ordinary files it named at the latest launch, read by the lead: "contains 'hypertension', which is a medical condition often documented in medical records"; "a school name, which is typical for a report card, indicating a financial statement"; "a financial estimate base year, characteristic of a tax return" -- topic words, on 89 to 400 characters of text, under a text that says "answer the nearest kind rather than none_of_these" when unsure. A v2 gate text is authored and registered UNRATIFIED (`gate_template.restricted-kind.v2.txt`): it asks WHOSE particulars the text shows; a paper, a dataset, a table, an exercise or a flyer that uses medical, financial or legal words is none_of_these; doubt goes to none_of_these because the file is read next by a second judge and is not sent. `GRAPH_AGENT_MEASURE_GATE_V2` points one launch at it so the gate records every answer and acts on none; run 11 (`run11-gate.sh`, on `measure.sqlite`, a copy of run 9's database) is that launch and its numbers are appended below.

**The judge, measured by replay.** A lead-only harness (`scratchpad/replay.py`) replays the 236 dossiers ALREADY SENT to the cloud -- read-only database, released evidence byte-identical to what crossed, answers cached by prompt hash, the private grading map -- so a text can be measured in ten minutes for about a dollar. The ratified text, replayed: right 129, wrong 41, silent 56, rejected 10 of 236 (54.7%; the stored answers of run 9 read 142 right of 235, the provider is not deterministic). The v2 text with the schema names on the menu (`recognition.compile` carries each schema row's own `name`, `SchemaRules.name`, `model_situation._candidate_items` puts it beside the id -- "nonprofit | Nonprofit, civic and member organisations | ..."), the owner's path sentence, no phantom `readings` key, a data table named as a dataset and a text-less image as a capture: right 149, wrong 44, silent 36, rejected 7 (63.1%): the club class 17 -> 22 of 37, the research classes 15 -> 31 of 46. A v3 text adding an audio recording as a capture, a résumé as career whatever its education section, and a course's notebook or dataset as academic: right 157, wrong 31, silent 38, rejected 9 (66.5%). What is left is not the text: the judge declines 10 audio files ("an audio file is not a photograph"), 9 dataset files shown only a header row, and 9 club-event photographs with no text and a path of "." that no judge can name; résumés and course notebooks are the wrong answers that remain. Two structural causes, measured: (1) the `photos` schema's `file_kind_never_alone` makes a JPEG or an MP3 with no words a `no_evidence` abstention (45 of the 50 text-less files), so the judge is asked about metadata alone -- a text-less capture is its kind, and is not a question for a model (agent, building); (2) the spreadsheet reader's unit is a CELL, so a dataset reaches the judge as 12-14 header cells of 5-30 characters and nothing else -- 25,339 of the corpus's evidence rows are cells, 45 dataset files have no fact; the unit becomes a row (agent, building). The 96% the owner asked for is not reachable by text alone on a flat folder of files with no words; the number of record after those two changes will be measured by the product's own run, not the replay.

**The facts column.** The cloud fact pass on run 9: 1,048 abstentions, 49 accepted claims. `subject` was declined 93 times as "not stated" -- and the ratified fact text (rule 13 of `a_fact_template_folder_levels.v4.txt`) requires a course CODE: "A title with no code anywhere in view is declined, not answered". The lead read eight such dossiers: the model names the course title it sees ("Honors Precalculus") and declines because no code is printed. On a person's own coursework a code is rarely printed on a problem set; the title is what they call the course. Proposed to the owner, not applied: `subject` may be the course's title where no code is in view, cited to the file's own line. The other declined fields (`term` 86, `work_type` 76, `capture_year` 45 on metadata alone) are honest declines on what the dossier carries.

**The four rulings, built and merged.** (4a) an answer is a learning record: one P15 event type `structural answer recorded`, `organization` joins `CORRECTION_SCOPES`, `apply_answers` appends the event for confirm, skip and revoke (d541baaa). (4b) `--file-held FILE_ID` writes `automatic_move_permissions[file_id] = True` on a new policy version and nothing else -- no classification row, so the cloud door does not open (04ede8a7). (4c) the prompt's path sentence, in the owner's exact words, in the v2 and v3 situation texts and the v4 shaping policy (unratified). (4d) the situation is not demanded before the judge: `_partition_branches` records the question, the run reaches the model pass, the question is printed at the end; exit code 0 where it was 2 (72f90ea8). (3) a schema the judge names no longer resolves to its alphabetically first situation: measured, no schema ships exactly one situation and only `nonprofit`'s name is a situation, so the resolution is the recogniser's raised situation where there is exactly one, else the person's answer; the fact pass asks the schema's fields meanwhile (agent, finishing).

**Later the same night (22:30-23:30).** The fact text measured by replay over the 223 fact dossiers already sent: with rule 13 relaxed so a course TITLE on a cover, a heading or a header line stands as `subject` where no code is in view (`a_fact_template_folder_levels.v5.txt`, unratified, in the scratchpad), `subject` is answered 13 times and declined 94, against 4 answers by the model on run 9; `term` (3 answered, 103 declined) and `work_type` (3, 102) do not move, and the lead's reading of the declines is that the text does not carry them -- a problem set prints neither its term nor a work-type word. The spreadsheet unit is a row (e131b1f2; `extractors.long_tail._rows_from_cells`, E3's version 0.4.0 -> 0.5.0 so every spreadsheet is re-read): on the synthetic corpus a 5x20 CSV went from 100 units of median 8 characters to 20 of median 44, and the released table evidence from fourteen cells carrying 100 characters to thirteen whole rows carrying 302, header first. The schema resolution (1af8529f): a judge-named file whose situation is open is asked its schema's fields through a level-less resolver (`model_facts.open_question` offers the schema's own fields where there is no situation to narrow by) and is not placed. The owner asked at 22:20 whether the local model (8 GB resident) can go: it is still the gate, the one layer that decides what may leave without sending it -- on this corpus the rules alone hold 15 of 28 protected and the gate caught about 12 more (boarding passes, health forms without a term, a screenshot); without it those go to the cloud on the rules' word. Their call; run 11 was stopped at 13 gate answers to give the memory back (its answers reuse on relaunch), and the number of record for the v2 gate waits for a machine the model can have. Full suite on the merged tip: see the last line of this section.

**A text-less capture is its kind (49535aa2).** `Detector._capture`: a file version whose live evidence carries nothing but filesystem, image or audio/video rows, and whose family in P5's recorded routing decision is image or audio/video, is recognised `photos` on its metadata rows, with no term; `settled_by_file_kind` is the predicate site G asks, and such a file is counted "settled by kind" and not put to a model (the seventh counter in the situation pass's partition). The agent's note, recorded as a consequence and not a change: such a file now carries an ordinary classification row where it carried none, so at sites A, C and D it is cloud-permitted where an unclassified file was local-only -- the cloud door still asks the gate pass which files it cleared, so nothing crosses on this row alone. Measured on the synthetic four-file corpus (a text-less JPEG and MP3, an OCR'd JPEG, a PDF): the two captures recognised and not asked, the two others asked as before. On the owner's corpus this reaches the 45 text-less files the judge was silent on; the number of record waits for the product's next run.

**What waits on the owner (13 Sep, 23:50).** (1) The gate v2 text and the judge v3 text and v4 policy: unratified rows measured by replay, one word each. (2) The fact text's subject rule (a course title where no code is in view): measured, one word. (3) Amendment 7(b)'s sentence, now "a person beside a diagnosis corroborates a medical term". (4) Whether the local model stays as the gate (12 to 13 protected files on this corpus rest on it) or the product runs cloud-only on the rules' word. (5) The ten key-protected files under the "all ordinary" note. (6) The next product run -- the gate under its true bound (run 11 resumes its 13 answers), the rules that release, the rows, the captures, the judge under whichever text is ratified -- and the review files regenerated from it; then the sort run.

**The naming-zone arm, found missing and put in (14 Sep 00:55).** The stale-pins agent noticed that a single filename term did not hold on the tip: the rule-N patch to the abstention arm had failed on a later anchor and written nothing, so the tree ran the two-signal rule there while the commit message and the design amendment described the naming-zone rule. Traced on the corpus: a file named for its verification codes, a prescription under its own heading, and a health form were free. The arm is in now. Measured against the CORRECTED key (20 protected after the owner's late rulings: two files named protected, ten called ordinary, the test-score report ordinary): protected held by the rules 16 of 20 (was 13); ordinary held 8 (two headings, two travel and household records the owner now calls ordinary, four datasets with a medical word in their filename or table) -- every one of the 8 becomes the person's question under amendment 2 of 13 Sep, and nothing among them is sent.

**Extraction coverage by format, measured (14 Sep 01:10).** Of 371 files: every PDF (131), DOCX (55), DOC (10), ZIP (12), IPYNB (7), XLSX (3), TXT, ICS, ODT and PPTX yielded text; CSV 50 of 51; JPG 23 of 38, PNG 12 of 18 and JPEG 2 of 5 yielded OCR text (the rest are photographs with no words, now settled by kind); MP3 0 of 16 (audio, no text, settled by kind). Not read at all, seven files: two notebooks saved under a misspelled extension (`.ipnb`), one SVG (XML text a reader could open), one WEBP and one GIF (images the OCR route does not take), one PSD, one INDD, one calculator program. Follow-ups, not built tonight: route `.ipnb` as a notebook, `.svg` as text, `.webp` and `.gif` to OCR. The merge of the held-record gesture (04ede8a7) was found to have merged the worktree's stale branch and six unrelated docs files; repaired (cc401028 carries the gesture, the six files removed).

**Cloud-only, the person as the second gate (14 Sep 01:30).** The owner: "don't use the local model unless absolutely necessary; use the cloud". Until now a deployment with a key and no local model did not run the gate pass and so cleared nothing: every file stayed home and nobody was asked. The pass now runs whenever its row exists; on a machine with no local model an un-held file is cleared on the rules' word (`GatePass.cleared_by_rules`, its own screen line, apart from `cleared`, which is a model's word), the held files are the person's question with `--release` and `--file-held`, and no gate dossier is built. Pinned end to end with the cloud stubbed: one file cleared on the rules' word, the held one named and never offered a cloud target. Two release pins fail only when run in isolation from their file (a fixture-order matter, recorded, not chased tonight). With this, the next product run needs no local model: rules -> the person -> the cloud judge under whichever text is ratified.

**THE BUILD ORDER FROM HERE (the plan, 14 Sep 00:40; `00` amendments of 13 Sep).** In this order, each gated by the one before:

1. **`--release FILE_ID` and the closing question** (agent, building): the held files named at the end of the run with both gestures; a released file carries a `user` row and the cloud opens to it next run. Done when its pin passes and the run's closing screen names the held files.
2. **The five stale pins** (agent, finishing) and the full suite green on the tip.
3. **The owner's word on four texts** (gate v2, judge v3, policy v4, fact v5 -- in `review/8_texts_to_ratify/`): each becomes a ratified row by editing its manifest status and nothing else.
4. **One product run, everything merged**, on the owner's corpus: the rules that release, the gate re-asked under its true bound (the local model resident for that one run, or -- if the owner runs without it -- the rules and the person), captures settled by kind, spreadsheets as rows, the judge under the ratified text, the facts under theirs. Grade against the corrected key (20 protected; the number of record for holds, the judge and the facts), integrity check, and the review files regenerated (files 1-6, and 7 for the held files the person is asked about).
5. **The owner answers the closing question and the branch questions** (`--release`, `--file-held`, `--answer situation:<schema>=...`), then one more run to apply them.
6. **The sort run** (`run10.sh`, ready; `score10.sh`): grouping, placement, the plan; the owner's review; freeze; move. Not before 1-5.

**14 Sep 2026, 00:20-01:40 -- items 2, 3 and the start of 4.** Full suite on 657f2ed8: 14 failed, 10,397 passed, 20 skipped, 33 xfailed (28 min). **Item 3, the owner's word: "ratify 1224"**, read as the four files in order and applied as NEW manifest rows (9925ad10): `gate.unratified.restricted-kind.v2.2026-09-14` (`ratified_local`), `situation.unratified.whole-library-v3.2026-09-14` with `situation_shaping_policy.v4.json` (`ratified`), `a_fact.unratified.folder-levels-v5.2026-09-14` (`ratified`); `A_FACT_ROW`, `SITUATION_ROW`, `GATE_ROW` re-pointed; `GRAPH_AGENT_MEASURE_GATE_V2` retired. The judge v3 row carries no `readings_rows`: the site's dossier never had the key and the text no longer claims it. **The fourteen failures were one design tension and one fixture fault** (4ea6ae8f): the 13 Sep closing question printed each held file's name on the plain report, and the owner's ruling of 2 Sep (`planning/93`, 710 names = 73 % of a report) is that protected names are summarised by default and printed by `--show-protected`. The question now stands inside that ruling: the count, what the two gestures mean and the command on every report; each file by name with its two typed commands under the flag, which reaches `run` for it. Six pins re-argued; the release pins had copied their database by file with the rows still in the write-ahead log (the "isolation-order flake" of 13 Sep) and copy through SQLite's backup now. **The gate header of a cloud-only run** named the cloud model as a model "on this device" (run 12's screen: "0 of 371 files were cleared by deepseek-chat on this device"); with no local client the header says no model was asked and counts the rules' clearances (ee2f2bc3).

**Item 4, run 12** (`run12.sh`, fresh `run12.sqlite`, cloud-only, no `--situation`, `--stop-after facts`, launched 00:40 from the ratify worktree with `.env` symlinked because the main tree was under the suite): the rules hold 24 of 371 (16 of the key's 20 protected; 8 ordinary), 347 cleared on the rules' word. Of the 4 protected files the rules miss (2 health records, a travel record, the arrival/departure record), 2 carry no classification at all and 2 a detector row with no safety evidence: on a deployment without a local model these cross to the cloud on the rules' word, which is what amendment 2 of 13 Sep says a cloud-only deployment does; the number is reported to the owner as the price of running without the gate. Judge, interim at 70 of 347 cloud dossiers: 52 right, 10 wrong, 8 none (74 %). The full numbers, the grade and the regenerated review files follow below when the run ends. Agent `e2e-sort` (Opus, `e2e/question-and-sort`) is building items 5-6's end-to-end on a synthetic corpus meanwhile: the closing question and both gestures, the branch question and `--answer`, the sort in run 10's shape.

**14 Sep 01:40-02:10 -- items 5 and 6 made true on a synthetic corpus (96d9a906, b665b6a1, 2e998f83).** The agent's three-run story (eleven files, six coursework, three of a student society in a subfolder, two the rules hold; cloud key, no local model, the cloud stubbed) found and fixed: `--file-held`'s move grant was overwritten fifteen milliseconds later by the same run's two policy writers, so the person's answer was undone and the file asked about again (`_permissions_in_force`, the grants carried forward; `_the_files_being_held(filed_by_hand=)`); the branch answer reaches its files (`selected_situation` at `branch:<schema>` read ahead of both library arms). **The blocker it pinned rather than fixed, fixed by the lead:** site C's route was the only one built without the gate's cleared set, so on a cloud-only deployment the placement judge could be asked about one file in eleven (the one the person released) and every other file abstained `no_model_judgement` -- item 6 was impossible on the deployment items 4-5 set up. `observe_placement_injections` now reads the same `cloud_cleared` as sites A, G and H; measured: the first run sends placement checks for the cleared coursework and none for the held files; the society's three files are not placed while their branch question is open (run 1's plan) and are filed under the society's own folder once the person has answered (the last plan). **The lead's own arm for the four protected files the rules miss:** `--file-held` on a file nothing holds writes the person's protected `user` row (`sensitive_personal`, `protected=True`), which shuts the cloud door because `model_route_permitted` refuses on the flag before it reads any basis; the closing question says so in one sentence. **One ruling left to the owner, pinned strict-xfail:** `--file-held`'s move grant is written at `cli.PLAN_VERSION` and P11 reads `automatic_move_permitted_for` at the run's own plan version, so the grant never reaches the read that decides whether a protected file may move. Amendment 2's words are "keep it here and you file it by hand", under which the product moves such a file never and the grant is moot; the other reading is that the gesture is the person's permission for the plan to move it. The owner's call; the lead's recommendation is the first reading.

**02:10-02:40, the agent's second pass (8dcb396f; `e2e/question-and-sort` 7c442192, 93dc1f9d, c522adc0).** Reading the owner's word of 01:00 ("follow the product design and fix all code"), the agent repaired both of its xfails instead of documenting them: site C's route threaded at both call sites (the lead had done the first; the merge kept one keyword), and `_permissions_in_force` made the one reader of `automatic_move_permissions` for all three policy writers, with a plan version that has no grants of its own inheriting the standing ones -- so `--file-held`'s grant is live at the plan version P11 reads and the gesture now means what its 13 Sep docstring said: the person's permission for the plan to move that protected file. **This is the ruling the paragraph above left to the owner, now applied in the gesture's original sense; one line reverts it if "file it by hand" means the person moves it themselves.** A third defect found and fixed: `apply_answers` recorded `correction_subject` as the question id under a scope whose subject is a branch, so `learning_records` at the branch found nothing; both columns now carry the scope's own subject. Twenty-one pins in `tests/integration/test_the_question_at_the_end_and_the_sort.py`, no xfail; the agent's wider runs: `tests/integration` 1437 passed, 20 skipped, 5 xfailed; `tests/p10 tests/p11 tests/p7` 1526 passed, 4 xfailed; the learning and correction-loop files 247 passed, 7 xfailed. Cost of the route on its corpus: 5 placement dossiers on a sort run and 8 on the run carrying the branch answer, against 0 and 1 before. Full suite on 8dcb396f: 2 failed, 10,451 passed, 20 skipped, 33 xfailed (28 min); the two were stale pins -- the A_fact row refusal names the row in force (v5 now), and the applied-answer learning record is found at the scope's own subject (the corpus) rather than the question id -- re-argued in the commit after.

**03:27, run 12 ended (exit 0 on the branch question, as designed: the folder's situation was unsettled, so the run printed its questions and returned before the facts stop).** Graded against the corrected key (20 protected): **protection** 17 of 20 caught (16 by the rules, 1 by the judge naming a protected schema), 3 missed (the boarding-pass image whose words are OCR alone, the arrival record, the zip with only a manifest), 13 ordinary files held (8 by the rules, 5 by the judge naming medical or identity), 2 key-protected files reached the cloud. **The judge** on 185 cloud dossiers: 137 right, 32 wrong, 15 none -- **74.1 %** (the replay of the same v3 text read 66.5 %; the difference is the product's own dossier and the captures settled by kind). By class: coursework 38/48, research papers 23/25, datasets 14/17, club records 26/38, recruiting 10/14, purpose packets 4/6, standardised tests 6/6, health records 4/7, online courses 5/8, programmes 1/4. Top confusions: club records to none or career (8), coursework to none (5), résumés and packets to academic (5), health records to academic (2). **Facts**: 162 of 371 files were asked nothing because their branch's situation is unsettled -- amendment 6's own order: the person answers the branch questions, the next run asks the facts. **Held files**: 30 marked, 24 put to the person; the 6 the judge protected are counted and not asked, because `HELD_BASES` does not carry the judge's basis -- a follow-up, since 5 of the 6 are ordinary by the key and the person has no gesture for them but `--release`, which refuses a file `HELD_BASES` does not cover.

**Two defects run 12's own screen showed.** (1) **The stale question list** (fixed, f-commit after this): the database recorded nine branch questions and the screen printed the first partition's two, the root's with its pre-judge count of 326. (2) **A third of the corpus was never judged, and the fix is the owner's.** Of the 347 files the rules cleared, 124 carry no classification row at all (nothing for the rules to say about them), and `model_route_permitted` refuses an unclassified file for the cloud before it reads the gate's cleared set -- a rule written for a deployment where nothing had judged the file. The local gate wrote an ordinary row for every file it cleared, so the arm never fired then; the rules write none. The arm that would make amendment 2 true here is one line in the door -- `if record is None and cloud_cleared is not None and cloud_cleared(file_id): return True` -- and the auto-mode classifier refused it as a privacy widening, which under the standing rule means the owner says the word, not the lead. Until then a cloud-only run judges only files the detector has read something from: 185 of 347 on this corpus, and the branch questions and the sort see only those.

**Review files regenerated** under `review/` (the previous set moved to `review/before-run12/`): file 1 carries `--file-held` for the three protected files the rules cleared; file 7 is the closing question with both commands per held file; file 4 says no gate ran.

**04:10, the judge's hold is the person's question** (the follow-up above, done): `HELD_BASES` carries `local_model_situation`, the closing screen phrases it ("held on the situation judge's word"), `--release` accepts it; pinned on the story's corpus. The stale question list is fixed (1e3d24c7). The full suite on the merged tip before these two commits was green but for two stale pins (c8893692); the two commits after it are covered by their own files, the gesture files, the byte-identity fixture and the coverage pins (35 and 290 passed). **What waits on the owner in the morning:** review file 7 (`--release` / `--file-held` per held file, thirty of them); file 1's three protected files the rules cleared (`--file-held`); the nine branch questions on run 12's screen (`--answer situation:<branch>=...`, or `--situation` for the folder); the door for unclassified cleared files (one line, refused by the classifier as a widening); the reading of `--file-held` as the plan's permission to move that file. Then one run to apply the answers, and run 10 (the sort) on run 12's database.

**Full suite on de30e3ec: 10,455 passed, 20 skipped, 33 xfailed, 0 failed (18 min).**

### 18.61 The morning of 14 Sep: the owner's readings and the next build

**The judge's 47 misses, read against the dossiers the judge saw** (aggregates; the lead read the dossiers): about 10 are volunteer, donation and charity paperwork the key calls club records and the judge calls "an organisation's administrative form that fits no situation" -- the menu's `nonprofit` has no word for volunteering (a menu name, the owner's to ratify); about 8 are essays, readings, a school schedule, a scripture passage -- "written prose, not coursework", right about the text, wrong only because the folder that says school work is not in a flat corpus (the recorded ceiling); about 10 are defensible either way (a clinical dataset from a course called research; an evaluation letter addressed to admissions called an application; application prompts called academic) -- the rows for the owner's "also right"; about 5 are the model ignoring the text's own rule (résumés listing AP courses called academic; a school immunisation certificate called academic); 3 invalid responses; 2 images answered `photos` while the cited reason said medical. Only 2 of the corpus's 48 photos were ever judged: the rest were among the 124 unjudged files.

**The owner's word, 10:30-10:50:** the door opens for rules-cleared files with no record (5d3290ec); cloud-only; the flash model where it is not used (measured first by replay on run 12's 185 dossiers, `replay-flash-v3.log`); file 7: travel and health kept, the others released (21 kept, 9 released; the two the 13 Sep word called protected stay held). **And the finding that changes the next build:** the nine branch questions are unanswerable as printed (`00` amendment 1 of 14 Sep) -- the judge must name the situation, and the flow becomes parse, gist, an editable proposed structure, then filing (`00` amendment 2 of 14 Sep).

**11:00-14:30, the flash model, run 13, and the provider's balance.** The flash tier measured 80.4 % by replay (148 of 184; 11 empty answers on the first ask, 7 of them answered on the second) and the cloud sites were moved to it (a3e51406). Run 13 (run 12's database, the door open, the 30 held-file gestures applied: 21 kept, 9 released) then built 170 judge dossiers and 96 came back with zero claims; the product's fact pass produced no usage in an hour. Two causes, found in this order: (1) the flash model thinks before it writes, and under the product's JSON mode and deadline it spends the ceiling thinking -- the provider's switch `{"thinking": {"type": "disabled"}}` is now in every request through the SDK's `extra_body` (the wire body still `request_body`'s output); (2) **the provider account's balance ran out** at about 12:33 -- every call after it is `402 Insufficient Balance`, which is also why the probe of the switch on both models failed. The tier switch is reverted (d5a8b184) until the switch is measured under the product's own request shape, which waits on the owner topping up. **The door had a second gate:** the release audit refused 124 rules-cleared files as `unreadable_unclassified` (built dossiers, no call) -- the rules-cleared arm now writes the ordinary row the local gate used to write (the commit after the reader's). Run 13 was stopped; its judge answers from the flash model (74 accepted) stand in the database and are re-asked under the model in force next run.

**14:00, the owner topped up; the flash measurement closed; the situation-level text measured.** Flash with thinking off by replay: 139 right of 185 (75.1 %), 0 empty answers -- level with the logic tier's model, so the owner chose the current model ("stay with the current model"). The situation-level judge (3bd6d45c, merged with the stage dark until its row is ratified; the lead's `prompt.ratified` gate at the top of `_ask_which_situation_of_the_kind`): v1 by replay on 89 of run 12's dossiers whose kind carries several situations, the menu the KEY's kind (the upper bound given a right first stage): 36 right, 17 wrong, 33 declined (40 %) -- the text declined when unsure and said the decline word was `none` while the vocabulary carried `none_of_these`; v2 (e67b5ad0: the kind is settled, choose; the menu's own word): 48 right, 17 wrong, 23 declined (54 %); the wrong are mostly siblings (coursework called k12-schooling 6, purpose packets called graduate applications 3). Put to the owner as text 5. The owner's order of 13:45: "condense the days into one, by tomorrow" -- agents `gist-structure` (00 amendment 2 of 14 Sep: the gist screen and the editable proposed structure) and `sort-freeze-apply` (freeze, apply, undo end to end on a synthetic corpus) dispatched in parallel; run 13 relaunched (the gestures were already applied by its first launch, so the run refused them once).
 The nine branch questions are in `review/10_branch_questions.txt`; review file 5's classification column reads in plain words.

### 18.62 The situation-level judge, iterated by its own reasons (14 Sep, 14:00-14:20)

The owner, on the level text's 54 %: "why is it only 54%? we need a much higher percentage." Re-tallied from the cache today, v2 stands at 44 right, 23 wrong, 22 declines of 89 (49 %); the earlier 54 % was the same text on a different set of answers (the model is not deterministic and the cache holds one answer per prompt hash), and the difference is noise around one number, not two texts. The judge's own reasons for every miss were read (the lead's harness, aggregates only) and gave three causes, all in the text or the menu, none in the model:

1. **"Record" read as "official document."** Fifteen of the coursework declines were readings and handouts -- textbook pages, articles, worksheets, study texts -- struck because no institution issued them. The lines say "record" and the judge read it as issuance.
2. **The text forbade the one question that separates siblings.** v2 said "do not reason about who the person is or what they were doing", which also forbade asking whether a file was given TO the person or made BY them as the instructor; eight coursework files went to the instructor's side or the institution's records about a child because a teacher authored them.
3. **A purpose-word capturing a named document.** Four resumes went to portfolio because that line says "framed to be shown" and the recruiting line, though it names the resume, lost to it.

Two texts and one set of menu lines followed, each measured by replay on the same 89 dossiers (run 12's cloud-released G dossiers whose kind carries several situations; stage 2 given a right stage 1; new prompt hashes, ~89 calls each):

| variant | right | wrong | declined | of 89 | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| v2 (14 Sep 13:40) | 44 | 23 | 22 | 49 % | |
| v3: given-and-made, whose-side, named-document, special-case rules | 51 | 23 | 15 | 57 % | declines down; K-12 confusions up (the NAME "K-12 schooling" pulls a secondary-school pupil's class work) |
| v3 + six one-lines rewritten in the harness | 62 | 13 | 14 | 70 % | recruiting 14/14; coursework 36/48 |
| **v4 (one paragraph: a published text or table kept is that work's reading or data) + seven lines** | **65** | **18** | **6** | **73 %** | |

Rows `situation-level-v3` and `situation-level-v4` are in the manifest, unratified; the seven one-lines are NOT in `recognition.json` -- they cross the internet in every level call, so they are the owner's, put to them as text 6 (`review/8_texts_to_ratify/6_one_lines.txt`, before and after), with text 5 now v4 (`5_situation_level_v4.txt` and its diff against v2). The 24 misses that remain under v4 are in `review/11_situation_level_disagreements.csv` (key situation, the judge's situation, its alternatives, its own reason, a ruling column), and they are of two kinds: about 11 where the judge names a more specific sibling than the key (mock exams of one test → standardized testing; statements of purpose to a research programme → graduate application; public statistics tables → reading library, after text 6 put "reports issued by an organisation" on that line) -- the owner's rulings, not text fixes; and about 13 where the released text carries no marker of any situation (a scripture passage, an opinion article, a photograph of a page: readings a flat folder gave no course to) or where the menu's own name pulls (five class worksheets to "K-12 schooling records" despite the rewritten line). If the owner rules the first kind "judge right" the stage stands at about 85 %; the second kind is the flat-folder ceiling and the name, and neither is the judge's.

What the number means in the product: this is stage 2 given a right stage 1. Behind the kind judge's 74 % (run 12), the end-to-end share of files whose situation the judge names right is the product of the two, roughly half; both stages need the owner's rulings on the "also right" rows before either number is the judge's fault. The stage stays dark (`prompt.ratified` gate) until the owner ratifies 5 and 6.

### 18.63 The two builders merged, and run 13's stall (14 Sep, 14:30-16:30)

**The situation judge's seventh commit** (3ae9a437, cherry-picked at 58645bd0): the locality gate is read per TEXT (`prompt_locality_permits`, off the definition a call is sent under) beside the site-keyed gate, because site G is the first site to ask under two rows; and `ask_the_situation` unwires the level stage for the run when its own row is unratified. The lead's per-file gate from the first merge stays under it as a second lock -- the auto-mode classifier refused its removal and the agent's test-file side (which deleted a dark-stage pin), and neither was reformulated; the tree's own pins assert the dark stage and pass.

**The sort, frozen and applied on a copy** (9ae08ae7, branch `sort/freeze-apply-end-to-end`): fifteen files, four situations; the person's `--release`/`--file-held` typed on one run and read three runs later; `--accept-groups` changes no file; `--freeze` writes the plan and one runnable line per branch; `--apply` moves exactly its branch, `--apply-everything` stops on the collision and skips the filed; `--undo`/`--undo-everything` put the folder back byte for byte. One privacy defect found by the corpus and fixed: the "new values the model proposed" block named whichever file a uuid sorted first, protected or not, five times on the plain screen (three runs in twelve); it now names a file that may be named, withholds name and gestures where every carrier is protected, and honours `--show-protected` (planning/93). Flagged, the owner's: the reading of `--file-held` (help text "keep it here" vs the code's grant to move), pinned both ways with one strict xfail; a reason string on unanswered branches that says the cloud "was turned away, did not return, or could not be checked" when in fact nobody answered the branch question (a wording change across sites, not fixed); "protected files never cross a folder boundary" not exercised by that corpus.

**The gist and the editable structure** (610a5242, branch `flow/gist-and-structure`, 00 amendment 2 of 14 Sep): `the_gist`/`_print_gist` after the scan and again with the judge's kinds (protected files counted on every line, named on none; a picture with nothing to read counted and said); `src/structure_file.py` and `structure_rows`/`_print_structure` under the level-names block, written beside the resolved database as `proposed-structure.txt`; `structure_edits` reads the file back as the gestures that exist -- a changed label is `--rename`, a deleted line `--reject` on every file the folder held, a new line ending `situation: <id>` is `--declare-role`, a `situation:` line under a branch is `--answer`; moves and edits no gesture can express are refused by name with nothing applied; an outline from an older proposal is refused by its stamped plan version. 23 pins across the two files. Flagged, small: no pin on the added-folder path; the branch fallback counts answered and unanswered `situation:` rows alike (a second open branch refuses saying two wait when one does); a deleted line whose files share a basename inherits `--reject`'s wording.

**Run 13 stalled in the fact stage, and the cause was one query.** The judge pass ended 14:17; by 16:14 the fact stage had built fifteen dossiers at 95 % CPU. A native stack sample: `SELECT length(text) FROM text_units` by primary key, `lengthFunc` walking every overflow page of the unit, asked by the fact builder's release check once per observation -- on a document of a few megabytes with thousands of readings, an hour of disk reads per file. The row stores its own `length`, equal to `length(text)` on all 23,360 rows of the run database; the lookup now reads the column (6a7e77e0; p4, p7 no-invention, file-released, stop-after-facts: 484 passed). Run 13 stopped and relaunched at 16:26 from the fixed tree (it re-asks the judge: verdicts are tied to their dossiers). The full suite runs on 6a7e77e0.

**16:30-19:00, the second stall and the green suite.** Relaunched on 6a7e77e0, run 13 stalled again in the fact stage (44 fact dossiers by 18:09), and the sample this time was `getOverflowPage` under `materialise`: `privacy.resolve.materialise` reads the whole text unit twice per item (`unit_for_observation`, `unit_holds_a_line_break`), and `gate._materialise` calls it once per item of a dossier -- thousands of reads of one multi-megabyte document. `materialise` now takes a per-pass memory keyed by the unit's own identity, which a `text_units` row never changes under; the gate makes one per call (f694a23c; p7, p9, no-invention: 1500 passed). Relaunched 18:14: judge stage replayed in 27 minutes (42 new dossiers, the rest read back from the database's own verdicts), fact stage at about two dossiers a minute. The full suite on 6a7e77e0 had seven failures: five were pins reading a screen that now carries the gist and the outline's path (re-argued at d9e954b0, with the unanswered branch's own reason word `situation_unanswered` and the structure file's `situation:` line counting open questions only), two were the reuse tool's fixture never having learned the eleventh identity term from 7f5deaa7, failing on de30e3ec as well (bf47ed27). **Full suite on f694a23c: 10,771 passed, 20 skipped, 35 xfailed, 0 failed (42 min).**

### 18.64 Run 13 done; run 14, the sort (14-15 Sep, 23:00-)

Run 13 (fourth launch, f694a23c, 18:14-23:07) ended on its own: judge stage in 27 minutes (42 new dossiers, the rest read back from the database's own verdicts), fact stage 209 files. Graded on the latest verdict per file: **judge 238 right of 316 cloud-sent files (75.3 %)**, up from 137 of 185 on run 12 because the door now lets every rules-cleared file through; protection 19 of 20 key-protected files held (the miss is a travel record the judge cleared), 8 ordinary files held (7 on the judge's word, 1 on a rule); 4 key-protected files reached the cloud (the four the rules do not catch; `--file-held` is the answer). Review files regenerated as `review/` with the old set in `before-run13/`; file 7 has 27 rulings. The fact stage still spent about half an hour on each of two 4.4 MB text files with 14,600 evidence rows each -- a third overflow-page walk under `run`, in a generator the C stack cannot name; a reproduction of the fact stage on a backup copy of the database, pointed at a dead local port so nothing leaves the machine, dumps its Python stack every 90 seconds and will name it. Run 14 -- the sort, run 10's shape on this database (`run14.sh`: `--accept-groups`, no `--apply`, no `--freeze`) -- launched at 23:08 for the owner's morning review.

**Run 14's first launch (23:17-23:55) ended after the facts with no group, no tree and no placement**: `downstream` returned nothing because NOBODY HAS NAMED THE SCANNED FOLDER'S OWN SITUATION. The judge gave 269 of 371 files their own situation, but the run's default -- the root branch, `Downloads` -- is settled only by `--situation` or by the root's own branch vote, and that vote offered three career situations "equally" for a mixed folder: the question the owner ruled unanswerable this morning, asked of the root. P8-P11 do not run without a default, so the sort never began (`groups` empty on this database across runs 12-14). GAP, THE OWNER'S MORNING READING: under `00` amendment 7 each file goes under a branch of its own situation, so a mixed root needs no default of its own -- its own unresolved files abstain -- and under amendment 2 the proposed structure is built from what was seen, not from one situation typed for the whole folder. Neither is wired: `going_on = stop_after is None and bool(of_the_run)` and `corpus_authorities is None` both end the run. For the morning, run 14 relaunched at 00:00 as run 10 ran, with `--situation academic.coursework` typed as the root's default (the owner's own word on run 10); every file the judge placed elsewhere still goes under its own situation. The screen of the first launch is kept as `scan14.out.no-root-situation`. The relaunch carries the lead's stack dumper on its path (a Python traceback to `scan14.stacks` every 90 s), so the fact stage's third overflow-page walk names itself if it recurs.

**00:30, the third walk named.** Run 14's relaunch carried the stack dumper, and at thirty minutes on the same screen it read: `unit_length_for_observation` (store.py:608) under `may_be_released` under `ordered_releasable_observations`, once per observation. The fix of 6a7e77e0 (read the stored `length` column) changed nothing on those rows: `length` is stored AFTER `text` in `text_units`, so reaching it walks the unit's whole overflow chain exactly as `length(text)` did. The release check only needs presence, and `SELECT 1` by the primary key is answered from the covering index and touches no page of the row (`unit_stands_at`, d5c47f2e; plan verified on the run database; p4, p7, p9: 1898 passed). Run 14 relaunched at 00:32. Still paying the same cost, and left for daylight: `extractors/entities.py:235` asks the length per reading during the scan, and the scan is replayed by the stat cache on every run of this database.

**00:50, the dumper crashed the run it had diagnosed.** Run 14's third launch (00:32, d5c47f2e) built 26 fact dossiers in thirteen minutes where the earlier launch built none, then died at 00:50 with no traceback: the crash report (`Python-2026-09-15-005048.ips`) is a SIGSEGV in `dump_frame` under `faulthandler_thread` -- the stack dumper's watchdog reading a frame the main thread was rewriting. Diagnostic only, never product code; removed from `run14.sh`. Fourth launch at 00:52 without it. Two lessons kept: `faulthandler.dump_traceback_later` is not safe to leave running on a long process, and the dumper's one read paid for itself -- it named in thirty minutes what two native samples and an hour of reading could not.

### 18.65 Run 14, the sort, done (15 Sep 00:52-01:10): what the owner will see

The fourth launch ran the whole sort: plan `version_f8188ae9_16`, 11 folders (8 proposed, 3 the owner's own), 371 files decided, **3 ready to file, 25 waiting for approval**, the rest abstaining; tree health 0-41 % per area; 12 questions; a proposed-structure file written beside the database. Screen and structure file copied to `review/12_sort_screen_run14.txt` and `13_proposed_structure_run14.txt`. Three things on that screen are the morning's work, all of them the product and not the corpus:

1. **The typed default bleeds into every branch.** The structure file prints `situation: academic.coursework` under photos, research, the owner's own resume folder and the root alike. Read at 01:30: `_the_situation_this_file_is_under` has two fall-throughs to the run's typed word. The first -- a file site G named for another kind, with no situation settled under it -- is closed (the run's word applies only to a file of the run's own kind; unpinned, because on the synthetic corpus such files abstain before the outline sees them). The second is the one run 14 shows: a file with NO named kind -- settled by the rules as a capture, or declined by the judge -- belongs to its root branch, and the root branch's situation IS the typed word, so a photo the rules settled sits under coursework. That arm is the design's own (`--situation` is the folder's default) and closing it is the amendment-2/7 question for the morning: a file the rules settled as one kind is not under the typed situation of another.
2. **The root question is still asked, now over 326 files** ("Which of these is Downloads? ... fit 3 of the situations equally", three career situations), with the typed default in force. A mixed root is not one situation, and the amendment-2 structure proposal is the form the answer takes; the branch vote over a root folder should not be a question at all.
3. **The tree is nearly empty** because placement abstains for most files: 154 settled by rule, the facts stage wrote few facts under the ceiling (21,175 readings left out of 118 calls on run 13's screen), and the top-level areas are the schema words (career, finance, nonprofit, photos, research) rather than situations. This is the state the owner is judging, and it is honest: nothing moved, everything is recorded, and the structure file is the seam they edit.

Run 13's numbers stand (§18.64). The fact stage no longer stalls (d5c47f2e). Tree at 35521227.

The owner, asked for their six decisions at once: text 6 ratified ("I hope you know what you are doing; ratify it") -- the seven one-lines written to their nodes under `planning/domains/nodes` with the replaced line and the ratification beside them, `recognition.json` recompiled from the nodes (7 lines changed; 85fa934c); file 11 "the key is correct" on every row, so v4's 24 misses are all misses and "73 % not good enough"; file 7: travel kept, "most are protected" (every key-protected file kept), two named files ordinary and released -- `run15-gestures.txt`, 22 kept, 5 released; and "have we not done classification yet, before sorting?" -- the order stands (00 amendments of 15 Sep, ac381312). Text 5 not ratified. ### 18.67 The kind judge iterated from its reasons; the ceiling of a flat folder (15 Sep, 01:45-02:00)

The owner: "solve the classification first before sorting; build, fix and perfect it tonight." The kind judge's 78 misses on run 13 (review file 3, the judge's own reason on each) fall by cause: 24 club and volunteer files (most of them photographs whose released values are camera metadata alone -- the judge says photos, the key says the club, and every one of them sits at the ROOT of the flat folder: 62 of 62 club files, 66 of 66 coursework files, 24 of 24 online-course files carry no folder path at all); 15 coursework files (photographs, scripture and readings with no course anywhere in them, a course cover letter read as an application); 9 datasets read by their subject (patients -> clinical, counts -> medical) or declined as bare tables; 9 online-course datasets read as research (they are course exercises, which only the owner knows); the rest one-offs. Kind text v4 (3003c503, unratified) adds six rules -- a capture whose recognised text is a record is that record's kind; a table is a dataset whatever its subject unless it is the person's own care, money or home; published works are a reading library; club paperwork is a nonprofit's record; course paperwork inside a school is academic; the person's own health forms are medical -- and measures on the same bench (run 12's cloud-sent G dossiers, the harness's own gloss of schema names): **v3 223 right / 45 wrong / 36 declined / 6 rejected of 310 = 71.9 %; v4 232 / 41 / 23 / 14 = 74.8 %**; v4 with a menu gloss naming clubs and volunteering on the nonprofit member 221 / 42 / 23 / 24 = 71.3 % (the gloss buys nothing and costs well-formedness). The bench runs about three points under the product (v3 in the product on run 13: 75.3 %). Put to the owner as text 7 (`review/8_texts_to_ratify/7_kind_v4.txt` and its diff).

**The ceiling, stated so the owner can judge it.** On this corpus every file is at the root of one flat folder. A photograph with no text is a photograph to every reader, and the key's "club" on it is the owner's memory of the event; a CSV of exercise data is a dataset to every reader, and the key's "online course" on it is the owner's memory of the course. No text put to a per-file judge reaches those, and they are about a quarter of the misses. What reaches them is the design's own next beat: the gist groups them (sixteen photographs from one day, seven data files with one naming shape) and asks the person once per group, or the structure edit does. The judge's ceiling on this corpus from bytes alone is roughly 85 % at the kind level; the rest is the person's, by design.

The level judge's five K-12 misses were measured against a renamed member ("School records a parent keeps about a child" in place of "K-12 schooling records", harness override, 02:05): 71 right of 89 either way, the same four files. The name is not the cause; the judge reads a school-issued evaluation form or schedule as the school's record about the student whatever the line says. No text 8.

### 18.68 Run 15, the first classification run with both judges live (15 Sep, 01:43-02:40)

Three launches: the first died in the live level stage on a `Refusal` from `run_call` read as a verdict (fixed 2b86393f, pinned); the second refused the file 7 gestures the first had already applied (the applied set kept as `run15-gestures.applied.txt`); the third ran to the end. Screen: 238 of 371 files given a kind by the judge, 168 of those asked their situation, 54 asked and left open, 16 not asked (one situation under their kind), 22 held (18 of 20 key-protected, 4 ordinary).

**Graded against the key at the situation level, the first time.** Read raw, 77 right of 168 = 46 %, and the reason was not the judge: **the key names situations in its own words** (`community.club-service`, `personal.media`, `research.paper`, `learning.online-course` ... 220 of 371 keyed files under ids the library does not carry), and the judge answers in the library's ids, so `academic.online-course` was "wrong" against `learning.online-course`. The bench never showed it because the bench skipped every key word the library lacks. The grade now reads each key word as the set of library members it means (`review/15_key_words_read_as_library_members.txt`, the lead's reading, the owner's to correct): **127 right, 22 wrong situation under a right kind, 19 wrong kind, 58 declined, of 226 answered -- 56 % of answered, 76 % of the 168 that named one.** The kind grade on the same run: 103 disagreements of 316 (67 %), down from 75 % on run 13 -- and the cause is INSTABILITY, not the text: the kind stage re-asked 250 files rather than replaying, and of 44 files answered in both runs, 18 changed their answer. The reader sets no temperature (`model_deepseek`'s own principle: no knob is a prompt nobody approved), so the provider's default sampling, 1.0, is in force. ccfada0a lets a caller name a temperature, absent by default; the bench measures both judges at 0 now. The 58 level declines: 26 are club files whose kind was named and whose menu -- the nonprofit members' lines -- describes an association's registers, not a student's club paperwork; a rewritten `nonprofit.student-organisation` line is on the bench beside it (text 8 if it moves). The bench itself now grades through the same key-word map, so club, media and paper files are on it for the first time.

**The four benches, 02:30-02:50.** Level v5 on the full bench (179 files through the key-word map): 124 right / 31 wrong / 24 declined = 69.3 %; with a rewritten `nonprofit.student-organisation` line 66.5 % (withdrawn); at temperature 0, 126 / 27 / 26 = 70.4 %. **Kind v3 at temperature 0: 189 / 85 / 35 / 1 of 310 = 61.0 %, against 71.9 % at the provider's default** -- sampling off makes the kind judge worse, not steadier-and-better, so the reader keeps no default and the optional term stays a bench instrument. Per class on the full level bench at 0: coursework 41/48, recruiting 14/14, standardised tests 6/6, datasets 16/17, online course 6/8; club 20/38 (17 declines: a student's club letters and notices fit none of the nonprofit members' registers, and the rewritten line did not move them), research papers 16/25 (7 read as datasets), health records 2/7 (5 read as a dependant child's -- the person is a minor, and the key's word may be the one to widen), application packets 0/6 (the packet is a keeping habit no text states). What is left for the judge at the situation level is about 30 files of 179, and half of those are the key's word against the library's for the same file.

That is what 62 of the owner's files are, and why 17 club files declined on the bench under every text. The two other seams are the key's word against the library's for one file: a minor's school health form (personal vs a dependant child's) and "a paper" (manuscript, reading, thesis) -- review file 15's rows.

### 18.71 Run 16 graded; the owner rules out abstentions (15 Sep, 13:05-14:00)

Run 16 (kind text v4 and the club member live, level v5, the file 7 rulings in the database) ended on its own at 13:50: 212 files given a kind, 161 of those asked their situation, 38 left open, 23 held. Graded through the key-word map: **135 right, 25 wrong situation under a right kind, 22 wrong kind, 48 declined, of 230 answered (58.7 %; 74 % of the 182 that named one)** -- against run 15's 127 / 22 / 19 / 58. Kind disagreements 92 of 316 (71 %), against 103. The owner, reading the declines: **"there should be no abstentions, make sure of that"** -- `00` amendment 5 of 15 Sep (ca7a7111): at classification a decline is a failure to judge, not a correct abstention; the judge names the plainest member and the person corrects it in one edit; §6.10 still governs placement. The decline word stays on both closed lists as a validation invariant (`SituationQuestion` requires the shortlist to end with it) and as the shape a malformed answer takes; the texts forbid it. Texts 10 and 11 -- kind v5 and level v7, the decline sections replaced by "Never" and what to do with a file that has nothing to read -- are on the bench.

**Level v7 on the full bench: 146 right / 32 wrong / 0 declined / 1 rejected of 179 = 81.6 %** -- no abstention left, and the best score yet (coursework 42/48, club 36/38, recruiting 14/14, datasets 17/17, reading library 4/4, standardised tests 6/6, online course 7/8; the three classes under half are the key's word against the library's for the same file: application packets 0/6, papers 15/25, a minor's health forms 3/7). Text 11.

**A bench defect found and fixed, 14:15.** `replay.py` took each file's LATEST site-G dossier, and since the level stage went live (run 15, 02:00) that is often a level dossier with a situation menu: the kind text was sent situation lists and answered in situation ids, graded as wrong. Two figures were contaminated and are withdrawn: kind v5 "64.5 %" (14:10) and **kind v3 at temperature 0 "61.0 %" (02:40, §18.68)** -- the sentence there that sampling off makes the kind judge worse is unproven until the re-measurement lands. The harness now reads kind dossiers only; both re-run.

### 18.72 The no-abstention texts measured; sampling off becomes the product's setting (15 Sep, 14:20-15:30)

On kind dossiers only (the bench defect of §18.71 fixed): **kind v5 (no decline) 235 / 52 / 1 declined / 22 rejected of 310 = 75.8 %; kind v5 at temperature 0: 246 / 50 / 1 / 13 = 79.4 %; kind v6 (v5 + exactly one citation) 243 / 59 / 1 / 5 = 78.4 %; kind v6 at 0: 257 / 51 / 1 / 1 = 82.9 %; kind v3 at 0, re-measured clean: 233 / 47 / 28 / 2 = 75.2 % against 71.9 % at the default** -- so §18.68's sentence that sampling off hurts the kind judge was the contamination, and is withdrawn. Level v7 at 0: 146 / 33 / 0 / 0 of 179 = 81.6 %, the same as at the default. The 22 rejects of v5 were citation discipline (seven stray brackets, eight metadata field names that were not the item's address, five references not on the list, five spans not verbatim); one citation and sampling off leave one.

**Chosen and applied (f-commit above §18.72's own):** `JUDGE_SAMPLING = 0.0` in the reader, on every cloud call, recorded as the twelfth call-identity dimension so every verdict taken under the default is re-asked. Texts 10 (kind v6) and 11 (level v7) in the review folder with a read-me; run 17 follows the owner's two words.

### 18.73 Texts 10 and 11 ratified and run; the wire, not the judge, is what run 17 measured (15 Sep, 16:00-23:30)

The owner at 16:00, shown both texts with their bench numbers and review file 15: *"this is good ... these are fine, they are close enough ... make sure there's no more abstentions and everything is properly like the product design labels."* Read as ratification of texts 10 and 11 and of the key-word map (`00` amendment 6 of 15 Sep, bc52863a): the kind judge runs under whole-library v6 and the situation judge under level v7, both with sampling off.

**Run 17 (15:58-22:44) is not a measurement of the judges.** 317 judge calls, 97 answers, **220 failures -- 207 of them `APIConnectionError`**, in bursts (41 in one minute, 60 in another) across seven hours, while a probe to the same provider succeeded immediately afterwards. The judge stage walks files one at a time, so this is not concurrency: it is the machine's network dropping in stretches. Its grade through the key-word map, which reads the database's latest answer per file and so still carries run 16's, is 139 right / 24 wrong situation / 23 wrong kind / 45 declined of 231 answered (60.2 %), and its screen's "43 of 371 given a situation" is the count of what THIS run could reach.

**Why no retry was built inside the transport.** `model_deepseek._send` sets `max_retries=0` on the owner's own argument (a retry multiplies a wait nobody chose, and P8 owns what happens to a failed call). The product's remedy for a lost call is its own next run: a prior verdict whose identity matches is replayed and costs nothing, so run 18 (23:17) re-asks only the files the wire lost. Run 17 replayed NOTHING because the `sampling` dimension had just changed -- which is the dimension working as intended, once.

**OWED, and named here so it is not lost:** a second sweep at the end of the judge pass over the files whose call failed at the wire, inside one run. Under the owner's no-abstention ruling a file left unjudged by a connection error is an abstention by another name, and "run it again" is a remedy the person should not have to carry out. `SituationPass` would carry the lost ids and `downstream` would re-ask that roster once.

**The eleven suite failures from the sampling change, fixed (5a3b9515).** The egress guard was right that `model_facts` must not import the provider module; the sampling now reaches the fact site as a field of `FactCallAuthorities` filled by the composition root. Two identity pins and the usage file's fake sender learned the twelfth term -- the fake had refused the keyword, so that test's run made no call at all. The owner's new member is the 361st node and the 317th `needs_llm` row, and it is refused as a TEMPLATE in the organisational library for its sibling's reason, written out: its second level is an occasion as often as a term, and `nonprofit` declares no key for a named occasion. Live for recognition, an honest gap for folders.

### 18.74 Run 18: the first clean product measurement under the ratified texts (15-16 Sep, 23:17-00:05)

Run 18 replayed what run 17 had answered and re-asked what the wire had lost. **516 answers, ZERO wire failures, and one decline in 358 judge answers** (kind 182 named, 1 declined; level 175 named, 0 declined, 0 malformed) -- the no-abstention texts hold in the product, not only on the bench. The screen: 224 of 371 files given a kind, 210 of those asked their situation, 3 left open.

**Graded over run 18's OWN answers, through the key-word map:** kind **148 right of 175 = 84.6 %** (run 13's ratified text: 75.3 %); situation **119 right of 168 = 70.8 %**. Cross-tabulated, which is what says whose error each one is:

| | situation right | situation wrong |
|---|---|---|
| kind right | 114 | 24 |
| kind wrong | 5 | 25 |

So 114 of 168 files are right end to end (67.9 %), 25 are lost at the kind stage and never recoverable at the level stage, and 24 are the level judge's own. **Half of those 24 are one defect, not a judgement**: twelve are the judge answering with the KIND's identifier ("nonprofit") where the menu holds `nonprofit.student-organisation` and its five siblings. The level dossier's `named_kind` item carries that identifier to say which list is being read, and the judge copies it; P8 rejects the answer and the file stays open, which is exactly what the owner's no-abstention ruling forbids. Level text v8 (unratified) says it outright in rule 1: the kind's identifier is not an answer, every member of `allowed_vocabulary` carries a dot, and the kind's own identifier is not in that list. On the bench next. The other twelve are real: six papers read as dataset analyses, two application packets read as the application they went to, and four singletons.

### 18.6 Stage 5 progress (9 Sep, 14:50)

Built by the lead while r18 runs: **S4** and **S5** (the commit above; 1015 p7 tests pass; `test_a_redacted_identifier_over_the_whole_document_is_refused_too` re-argued: under a classifier that names no class the always-local refusal precedes the whole-document one). **S6 deferred, not built:** `template_for` has no producer anywhere -- the per-file template is site E's answer and E is unratified -- so wiring it today would pass a function that returns `None` for every file, which is the same dead arm with a different spelling; it is E's ratification that makes the arm live, recorded here so the arm is not deleted in the meantime. **Awaiting the owner's word:** S1 (redacted-prompt option), S2 (class default `pending` = zero cloud coverage until a kind recogniser writes the column), the relative-path release, gap 7 and gap 8 (manifest rows), the protected-files ruling, and the eight evolution sentences. Gaps 4, 5, 9, 10, 13, 15 (S) are next for agents once r18 has the machine to itself no longer.
